# Known issues (written for 0.1.0-alpha.1, re-checked on 2026-11-12 for the alpha.3 line)

This is the honest list, written on 2026-10-19. It comes from the "Not verified" and "Known gaps" sections of the README, the "Unverified" notes in
`docs/`, and every row of [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) that is not `done` (the full generated list is the appendix at the end).
If something is not here and it does not work, please report it: see [SUPPORT.md](SUPPORT.md).

**The one sentence to remember (re-checked 2026-11-12):** IYAKKI has been run in QEMU, in a desktop window and in containers, and on real computers: a Mac mini 2018
(Macmini8,1, T2; since 2026-10-18), a 2020 MacBook Pro with the T2 chip (MacBookPro16,2; USB SSD and its internal disk), and, for the alpha.2 images, a MacBook Pro 14,1,
an HP Pavilion 16 laptop (Intel + NVIDIA) and a Lenovo laptop (the owner booted the final alpha.2 image on all four on 2026-11-10; see
[RELEASE-NOTES-0.1.0-alpha.2.md](RELEASE-NOTES-0.1.0-alpha.2.md), "What was tried where"), plus **one AMD PC** that booted (nothing else about it was written down). What worked on each machine
beyond booting is recorded only where a KI below or a row of [FEATURES.md](FEATURES.md) says so. It has not been run on any other PC or Mac, a managed cloud cluster or against most vendors' live
services. The table is in [docs/HARDWARE.md](docs/HARDWARE.md). **This alpha is a preview for developers and testers.**

## Do not use it for

* KI-001 **Production, or any machine that holds data you cannot lose.** The installer (`iyakki-usb --install`, and `iyakki install`) erases the
  whole drive you choose. The data volume is **not encrypted** (vaults inside it are). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true. Since alpha.2 a person can make an encrypted home folder (one folder per account, one real-hardware run); the data volume itself is still not encrypted, and the installer still erases the whole drive you choose.
* KI-002 Production clusters. The deploy tools refuse profiles that look like production, but the cluster side was exercised against fake
  `kubectl` runners and local K3s (including the dev cluster on the Mac mini), never a managed cluster (EKS/GKE) or a real remote cluster. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: no managed or remote cluster has been used; only fake `kubectl` runners and the local k3s on the Mac mini.
* KI-003 **Secure Boot on real hardware.** Secure Boot (shim + MOK, [docs/SECURE-BOOT.md](docs/SECURE-BOOT.md)) was verified in QEMU with OVMF only (signed
  GRUB, kernel and modules boot, unsigned ones are refused; 17 checks). It was **not tried on any real PC or on a T2 Mac**, and it needs the
  owner's key and its enrolment. Images are signed only when the owner builds with `IYAKKI_SB_KEY`/`IYAKKI_SB_CERT`; otherwise they are
  unsigned and need Secure Boot off. The initial RAM disk is not signed; GRUB 2.12 lacks the 2025 security fixes; driver packs with unsigned
  modules are refused under Secure Boot. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: no Secure Boot result on a real PC or a T2 Mac is recorded in the repository (the alpha.2 hardware sheet only asks whether the signed loader boots); QEMU with OVMF is the only proof.
* KI-004 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** Anything exposed to the internet. The browser front end speaks HTTPS with a self-made certificate and has had no outside review. The
  in-cluster registry one-click (private store) opens the registry to the network on NodePort 30500 **without a password**.
  Fixed in the tree 2026-10-28: `localreg::tests::the_registry_manifest_has_no_network_exposure`, `localreg::tests::an_existing_nodeport_registry_is_converted_to_loopback_and_audited`, `regsetup::tests::applying_over_an_open_nodeport_registry_closes_it_first`, `k3sdev::tests::a_cluster_that_becomes_ready_closes_an_old_open_registry_and_audits_it`; verify on the image: `curl http://<ip>:30500/` must fail from another computer. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the registry part is fixed in the tree (the tests above still pass); the claim "no outside review" is still true. **Hardware/QEMU check:** on an image with the dev cluster and the private-store registry on, run `curl http://<ip>:30500/` from another computer; it must fail (connection refused).

* Linux windows / Linux apps on an image (2026-10-29): the first Mac run failed with `linuxapp-init: pivot_root: Invalid argument` because an image's root is the initramfs. Fixed in the tree (enter by mount move and chroot on `rootfs`); proven in QEMU on an initramfs; **run on the Mac on 2026-10-30 and the install still failed** (KI-075), and `apt` inside the sandbox on an image has never completed. The isolation on `rootfs` is a chroot over a covered mount, not a replaced root (docs/LINUX-APPS.md).

## Hardware: what was tested

**Tested on real hardware, machine 1 of 3:** a Mac mini 2018 (Macmini8,1, T2; Intel UHD 630; 32 GB), booting the macweb install image from a USB
SSD (Samsung T7), with an HDMI monitor, Ethernet, a USB keyboard, mouse and webcam, on 2026-10-18/19. What worked there: the stick written
by `iyakki-usb` (GPT, read-back verified), booting through GRUB on UEFI, the boot screen, the data volume and persistent `/home`, HDMI audio
and the built-in speaker and headphone jack (through the out-of-tree `apple-bce` driver), software volume and mute, the USB webcam, the
clock and time zone, the k3s dev cluster and the other Quick install items, Claude Code and GitHub Copilot CLI install and sign-in,
Lemonade on the LAN, and the full web engine (Google, YouTube with VP9 video). Details: ALPHA-GATE.md items 46 to 54.

**Machine 2: a 2020 MacBook Pro with the T2 chip (MacBookPro16,2), owner report, not in `ALPHA-GATE.md`.** Booted from a USB SSD (Samsung T7):
keyboard and trackpad, speaker sound (through `plughw`; the shared mixer is off by default and not run on it), the GPU browser helper, YouTube autoplay and
sound, page fullscreen and the Touch Bar backlight worked. On **2026-10-24 the first install to the internal disk booted**. Still open there: the
IYAKKI-drawn Touch Bar strip never opens (the USB configuration switch falls back), the built-in microphone is off on purpose (capture has hung the
machine), Bluetooth (BCM4364: no patch file exists; fails), upgrading that install, battery (kernel options not yet in an image), sleep, Secure Boot.
**Seen on 2026-10-26/27 (coordinator, through developer mode; no saved diag file):** the macweb image booted from the internal disk, k3s Ready (v1.37.1) and started
again at boot, the internal panel (2560x1600) and a DisplayPort monitor (1920x1080) were driven, Wi-Fi works with Apple's firmware, the built-in camera works, T2 audio works,
developer mode works over Wi-Fi. Built and **not yet verified**: the touch panel on the monitor hub reacts on the wrong display, the camera picker, developer mode blocked
by the device firewall on port 7979, TALES highlight drift inside sentences, the dev cluster after an address change. Per-capability table:
[docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md](docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md).
The boot drive (a T7 over USB-C) vanished once for 0.6 s (F-LOSS-01 to F-LOSS-06).

**Machine 3: an AMD PC, owner report only.** It booted. The model, the GPU, and whether sound, network, input and an install worked are **not
recorded anywhere in the repository: untested in detail** until a hardware report is saved (`iyakki hwreport`, [docs/BOOT-TEST.md](docs/BOOT-TEST.md)).

**Planned, no result yet (PC image built 2026-10-26, tests 2026-10-27, QEMU boot only so far):** an Intel + NVIDIA PC and an AMD + NVIDIA PC (the owner reports the AMD one booted; no log). NVIDIA cards run on `nouveau` (Turing and newer) unless the person
installs the vendor driver ([docs/GPU-K3S.md](docs/GPU-K3S.md)).

**Expected to work, but untested:** other Intel PCs, laptops and mini PCs (UEFI or BIOS), Intel Macs without a T2 chip, the Mac mini 2014,
other USB cameras, sound devices and drives. `docs/BOOT-TEST.md` is the guide and `iyakki hwreport` is how you tell us what happened.
(F-BOOT-01)

**Not working or not verified, on the Mac mini 2018 and elsewhere:**

* KI-005 **Wi-Fi of the Mac mini 2018 (Broadcom BCM4364) does not work yet.** It needs Apple's firmware, which IYAKKI never ships. The IYAKKI
  menu, Mac drivers page now installs it at your request: **Download from Apple** (the recovery image of your Mac model, read with 7-Zip),
  or from a folder, a stick or a disk image (the macOS script `dist/drivers/mac-wifi-firmware-from-macos.sh` still works). The file
  names the kernel asks for are verified on the Mac mini, and on 2026-10-21 Apple's recovery download worked and 7-Zip read the image there; **the install and the result have not yet been seen on the Mac**; the recent macOS image has **no Bluetooth patch file for the BCM4364** (the job then tries the other macOS version Apple offers) (a numbered real-device checklist is in `docs/MAC-WIFI-BLUETOOTH-SETUP.md`). Until
  the firmware is installed the Wi-Fi and Bluetooth drivers of a T2 Mac are meant to be held back so the kernel log has no error spam (the first device run showed the blacklist alone did not hold; the module files are now moved aside too, not yet retried). Use Ethernet
  or a USB adapter meanwhile. On the 2020 T2 MacBook Pro the same firmware route worked (Wi-Fi up with addresses by DHCP, 2026-10-26/27, observed through developer mode); on the Mac mini 2018 no
  adapter has associated yet and the run must be repeated there. The Mac mini 2014's BCM4360 needs Broadcom's non-free `wl` that you build yourself (`dist/drivers/build-wl-pack.sh`); never
  loaded on a real Mac. (F-NET-02, F-DRIVERS-*) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true for the Mac mini 2018 (no Wi-Fi association recorded there); for the 2013 to 2015 Macs see KI-094.
* KI-006 **Bluetooth is limited on the Mac mini 2018.** Its BCM4364 has no firmware file in macOS (measured: neither the latest nor the other
  macOS version Apple offers contains a BCM4364 patch file, and there is no legal source), so its controller runs in its ROM state
  (limited): it registers as `hci0` but has no valid address. Settings → Bluetooth shows that (controller, address, powered on/off,
  reason), can power it on and scan for devices for 10 seconds as proof it works; the Bluetooth drivers stay set aside until the
  person presses "Load the Bluetooth driver anyway" (Mac drivers page, Devices) or a Bluetooth driver pack is installed. IYAKKI has its own
  Bluetooth stack now (pairing with passkey or PIN, keyboards and mice, phone sound, tether): pairing, phone sound and tether with internet were tried on a
  MacBook Pro 14,1 (2026-11-08); on the Mac mini 2018 the new page scans and lists devices (2026-11-10) and pairing is untried. A USB Bluetooth adapter is the
  reliable option there. Low-energy devices and a real PIN device are untried anywhere. (F-NET-11, F-NET-12) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-007 **The newest image is not confirmed on the Mac.** The fix for a 5.3 s input-device scan stall at boot and the shared sound mixer fix (YouTube
  had no sound while the radio played) are in the images since (the final alpha.2 image, build alpha2c / 1109a, boots and runs on the Mac mini 2018 and three other computers, owner report 2026-11-10); the 5.3 s scan time and the mixer fix themselves were not measured separately, so this entry stays until someone reads the boot log and plays YouTube while the radio plays.
* KI-008 **The boot is slow at the start.** On the images measured in 2026-10 GRUB waited 20 to 30 s at the logo (the screen says 'please wait 30 s to 1 min') while the 160 MB initramfs
  is read through the Mac firmware's USB driver; a side image on the boot partition is planned. The first picture of the shell comes about
  9 s after the kernel starts, then the login. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the first sentence describes GRUB. Since 2026-11-07 the own boot loader is the default of every UEFI image build (`docs/BOOT-LOADER.md`, F-BOOT-20 and following) and the alpha.2 images boot on four computers, but nobody has timed the wait with it on the Mac mini, so the 20 to 30 s figure is neither confirmed nor withdrawn. **Hardware check:** power on a Mac mini 2018 from the alpha.3 T7 stick and time power-on to the first IYAKKI picture and to the sign-in screen.
* KI-009 **T2 MacBook Pro:** booted (see "Machine 2" above) for the 2020 MacBookPro16,2. The 2019 model and the other T2 MacBooks (2018 to 2020) were not tried. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true for the 2019 model and the other T2 MacBooks. The MacBookPro14,1 (2017, no T2 chip) booted the alpha.2 image and had Bluetooth, phone sound and tether tried on it (owner, 2026-11-08/10).
* KI-010 **Apple Silicon:** the way is a lightweight VM with k3s and a bridge to the model runner (owner decision 2026-10-27); **bare metal is out of scope and not promised**
  (F-BOOT-09, F-DRIVERS-12 and F-DRIVERS-19 are `deprecated`). The VM recipe has never been run on a Mac (`docs/APPLE-SILICON.md`). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (no Apple Silicon run; `docs/APPLE-SILICON.md`). The macOS desktop window (KI-078) is separate and does run on an arm64 Mac.
* KI-011 **ARM64:** the image boots in QEMU only (software emulation, slow); no ARM64 hardware, cluster or CLI install was tried. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-012 **Bare-metal PCs:** one AMD PC booted (no details recorded), and since 2026-11-10 an HP Pavilion 16 (Intel + NVIDIA; it needed a kernel with the x2APIC options, see `docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md`) and a Lenovo laptop boot the alpha.2 image (owner); the HP camera worked. What else works on them (touchpad, sound, Wi-Fi, GPU) is not recorded, apart from the problems found in KI-080 to KI-082; all other PCs are untested. Touchpads, touch screens and GPUs other than the
  Mac mini's Intel UHD 630 (and the MacBook's integrated GPU) were never recorded as tried; touchpad support on bare metal is planned. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** reworded above. **Hardware check:** run CHECK-UP (`docs/CHECKUP.md`) on the HP and the Lenovo and save the result file; each pass or fail closes a cell of `docs/project/HARDWARE-MATRIX.md`.
* KI-013 **Microphone and the Mac's own FaceTime camera:** on the T2 MacBook Pro (2026-10-26/27, observed through developer mode) the built-in camera works at 640x480 YUYV and an
  external USB microphone works (owner); the built-in microphone of T2 Macs stays off on purpose (capture hung the machine). Fixes built and not yet verified there: the camera
  picker, the touch panel mapping on a second display, developer mode behind the device firewall (port 7979). Not tested on any other machine.
  **Browser microphone (2026-10-28):** on the T2 MacBook the web engine's own ALSA microphone path failed (`Rate doesn't match (requested 44100Hz, get 0Hz)`, then not-negotiated; a page recorded a flat line). Reproduced on a PC: a raw `hw:` capture at a rate the hardware lacks is not-negotiated, `plughw` converts. A new microphone plugin (`libgstiyakkimic.so`) lists the capture streams itself and opens them through `plughw`; the Browser's microphone question has a Microphone chooser that is the same choice as the Voice tile's. Written and tested with fake devices and a PC sound card, **not yet run on the Mac**; the T2 card's microphones also need the `apple_bce` capture switch (`echo 1 > /sys/module/apple_bce/parameters/capture`, off by default), whether `Digital Mic` delivers sound there is unproven, and the engine needs the media-stream rebuild (F-BROW-72). The helper logs one `iyakki-mic:` line per request for the next diagnosis. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the camera light problem is now KI-080/KI-093 (fixed in code, pending on the Lenovo); the browser microphone fixes below are still "not yet run on the Mac" except what KI-069 says was confirmed with hot patches.
* KI-014 **The `hw` image** (firmware blobs) was never booted on a real machine. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-015 **Display:** bare metal uses the fbdev framebuffer (no DRM/KMS), software rendered and single-threaded. Video in the web engine is
  decoded in software: a 720p YouTube video dropped about 57 of 628 frames, 1080p60 drops more. Very large UI sizes on small screens overflow
  many pages. **Overflow harness (2026-10-28):** `monkey_tests` draws every tile and each of the 31 Settings pages (and the Store's Setup tab) into the test
  buffer and reports text that ends beyond the window, beyond the button or field it starts in, or beyond its pane, and texts that cross each other. Three tests:
  `overflow_every_tile_at_the_largest_text_size` (Huge, 200%, in 360x740, 800x480 and 1366x768), `overflow_every_tile_at_laptop_and_large_screens`
  (1280x720 and 2560x1600 at Normal and Larger text; about one minute) and the ignored `overflow_every_tile_at_every_size_and_text_size` (all five sizes at all five text
  sizes). Each fails on a finding that is not listed in `KNOWN_OVERFLOWS` / `KNOWN_OVERFLOWS_MATRIX`. To print everything as a table of page, size, kind and text:
  `IYAKKI_OVERFLOW_REPORT=1 cargo test -p iyakki-shell --lib overflow_every -- --nocapture` (`IYAKKI_OVERFLOW_ONLY=settings/Vault` keeps one page). **Keyed by page name (2026-10-29):** the known lists are keyed by the Settings page's name (`settings/Vault`), not its place in the list, so a new page cannot shift them; the test walks every page the account has. Pages added later than the first runs (SSH tunnels, VPN, the Browser custom-headers sub-page) were made to fit at 800x480, 1366x768 and 1280x720@150%; SSH tunnels and VPN still overflow at 360x740 for the reason every Settings page does (the page list takes the width). Git and SSH keys and Clusters were never reached by the old 31-page loop and are listed as found (360x740, 800x480, 1366x768). Also fixed: Browser 'Accept cookies' at 1280x720@150% (shorter button labels), the empty-state lines of Vault, Git and SSH keys, Last session log.
  **Fixed from the first runs:** the launcher's key hint over the host name; an input's hint running out of its box (Store and Media search); the Settings page list drew
  a half-visible row over the window edge (now clipped to the list; this was 15 pages at 1280x720 and 7 at 2560x1600); one-line explanations of Secret providers, Agents and
  Skills and the Skills preview's `triggers:` line are shortened to the page width; the Skills detail pane's "lines x of y" hint no longer shares a row with the text.
  At Normal text size 1280x720 and 2560x1600 have no finding; Larger text (150%) at 1280x720 has the five pages below, 2560x1600 none.
  **Still open** (the table lists every page and size; a page with several findings shows the first two; the numbers are findings, not pixels): below 1366 wide
  most Settings pages have long explanatory lines and label/value rows that are not wrapped to the width; at 1280x720 with Larger text the content of five Settings
  pages is taller than its room and runs into the button bar. The detector is a heuristic and was not compared with a real screen; sizes below 360 wide (such as
  200x150) are out of scope. The overlap rule ignores two clock readings in one place (the same clock read on both sides of a minute change). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the overflow table below was generated on 2026-10-28 and has not been regenerated; pages added or changed since (SFTP controls, Matrix mirror button, the docked IDE Preview) have their own size tests but are not in the table.

* KI-016 **Boot time and size:** about 1.6 s to the sign-in screen in QEMU (the Mac mini takes much longer, see above); the spec's 30 MB / 100 ms
  targets are not met (the libre ISO is about 60 MB; the web image about 2 GB). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** not re-measured since 2026-10; the figures are dated, not current.

* KI-160 **First USB-C plug of a 4K monitor listed only 1080p (Mac mini 2018, kernel 6.12, i915; found 2026-11-12).** The connector `DP-1` was `connected` and its `edid` file held a valid 256-byte EDID (preferred timing 3840x2160 at 60 Hz, 4K modes in the CTA block), but `modes` listed only the generic fallback set (1920x1080 and smaller), so Settings -> Display offered nothing bigger and the shell ran at 1920x1080. After a second cable on another connector was plugged and removed, the same USB-C cable gave 3840x2160 with no software change. Cause not proven (the kernel's mode list was stale or pruned at the first hotplug, or the link trained low). Recovery added (F-DISP-13): a connected display whose mode list lacks the EDID's preferred size, or whose EDID is empty or short, is asked again by writing `detect` to its `status` file at boot, on every display event and on Settings -> Display -> Look again (also the chat tool `displays_look_again`), three times with waits of 1, 3 and 8 seconds, in a background thread, one `iyakki.display_retry` line per attempt in the log; the screen is drawn again at the best mode. Tested with fake sysfs folders only; the hardware re-test (unplug, boot with the monitor unplugged, plug it in) is still to be done. The KMS GETCONNECTOR fallback is not separate code: the shell's own once-a-second rescan already re-probes the connectors when it drives several displays.

## Browser and media

* KI-017 **H.264 and AAC video does not play, on purpose.** They are not shipped (patent policy). TikTok loads but its video does not play.
  YouTube plays VP9 + Opus. Hardware decoding through VA-API is an idea for later. Playback is software-rendered. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true as the default. KI-070 describes the add-on route (AAC and H.264 only after the administrator installs an add-on).
* KI-018 The media player is built on `ffmpeg` (a static build downloaded from the vendor at your request, never shipped). It was tested with fakes and
  with a real file decode, but **not yet confirmed on screen**. The same `ffmpeg` decoded the camera's AVI on the Mac mini. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: no on-screen confirmation is recorded.
* KI-019 The small built-in browser (non-web images) has no video decoding, no canvas, no IndexedDB, no PDF viewer. Chinese, Japanese, Korean and
  emoji text shows as boxes. See `docs/BROWSER-ROADMAP.md`. The full web engine (WPE WebKit) is in the large web and macweb images only; it opened
  Google and YouTube on the Mac mini and in QEMU, but was not exercised across many sites (`docs/BROWSER-AGENT.md`). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-020 **YouTube video and big pages are slow in the full engine on Macs, and this was measured.** Loading takes 2.6 to 5.3 s; what is slow is
  scrolling and video: on the web and macweb images Mesa has no Intel driver, so WPE composites with llvmpipe on the CPU (a 60 frames a
  second 720p clip: 6 to 8 pictures a second, 61 to 80 % of its frames dropped; 1080p about 2). **Settings -> Browser -> Smooth pages**
  (off by default; half the pixels, softer text) gave 27 pictures a second and no dropped frames on the same clip, and a 24 fps 1080p
  clip played at its own rate; half of the CPU threads for llvmpipe stopped the dropped frames (both need a new `iyakki`, not the image's
  engine). While any video plays one thread of the web process uses a whole core because the T2 audio device runs with a one-frame period
  (`avail_min 1`): not fixed. The real fix, the Intel GPU driver (`WEB_IRIS=1`), is not in any image. The shell's own drawing with a live
  tile was not measured, and the tile-only painting that should speed it up has only been tested, not run on a screen. Brotli and WOFF2
  fonts are still off (`docs/BROWSER-SPEED.md`, "Interactive performance"). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true; the `WEB_IRIS=1` driver is in no image.
* KI-021 **Ad and tracker blocking** (Settings, Browser): lists are downloaded by the person and are other people's work; the full engine's
  rule loading was never run against a real WPE WebKit and its helper code was not compiled yet; the full engine reports no count of
  blocked requests; about a fifth of the path rules of the real lists do not fit WebKit's limit; YouTube video ads are not promised
  to go (`docs/CONTENT-BLOCKING.md`). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true. KI-086 covers the headers extension, which is also not compiled against real WPE headers.
* KI-022 **Radio formats:** MP3 streams and HLS stations with MP3 segments play with in-house code. AAC streams and HLS stations with AAC segments need the
  user-downloaded audio add-on (without it the station says so); the decoder path is tested with a fake decoder only, not with a real ffmpeg on the
  hardware. Ogg Vorbis/Opus streams and a station directory search are not built. (F-RADIO-03, 05, 06)
  Tests: `radio::tests::an_hls_station_with_mp3_segments_plays_without_the_add_on_and_follows_the_live_edge`, `radio::tests::an_hls_station_with_aac_segments_needs_the_add_on_and_then_feeds_it_plain_adts`. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: F-RADIO-03 is partial 30/50, F-RADIO-05 and F-RADIO-06 are tested with fakes only.

* KI-096 **Fixed in code 2026-11-12, browser re-check pending: the committed client engine module was older than its source, so the camera list of the browser client never worked.** The module was rebuilt with `sh dist/client-engine/build.sh` (sha256 7a23319070731bf6c67), the browser suite (`dist/browsertest/run_pages.py`) reports the check as passing once it is run again, and the marker in `clientsuite.py` can then be removed. Found 2026-11-12 by running the engine in Chrome (`dist/browsertest/run_pages.py`, section client-media): commit 87453fd added the `camera_list` command to `crates/iyakki-client-engine/src/{engine,host}.rs` and to `js/media.js`, but `crates/iyakki-client-engine/assets/iyakki-client-engine.wasm` was not rebuilt (it is from 705f954, which is older), so the module answers `{c: "camera_list"}` with a refusal and opening the Devices panel never asks the machine for its cameras. The gate's `dist/client-engine/build.sh --check` compares export lists only, so it did not notice. Fix: run `sh dist/client-engine/build.sh` where the wasm32 target and node are installed (neither is on the Mac used for the browser run) and commit the module; then remove `known="KI-096"` from `dist/browsertest/clientsuite.py`. (F-CLIENT-05, F-CLIENT-07)
* KI-660 **The engine's own response headers are written, not run (F-BROW-75, 2026-11-13).** The helper reports the headers of a committed page (`dist/webview/wpe-helper/helper.c`, `send_response_headers`, message 0x93) and `browser_headers` answers from them while developer tools are on. The protocol, the shell and the tool are tested with fakes; the C was not compiled (the build machine has no WPE headers) and no real engine has sent the message. Until it does, the tool sends its own request as before.
* KI-661 **RTSP over UDP and the media server's subscriber list were tried only on the loopback address (F-CAM-15, F-MEDIA-02, 2026-11-13).** A camera that answers `461` to the TCP request is asked for UDP; no real camera has done so. The Settings page lists the players that follow the media server for changes; no real player (a TV, VLC, a phone app) has subscribed. Cameras that need `https` or `rtsps` are still not supported (a decision about self-signed camera certificates is needed first).
* KI-663 **A changed custom-header rule does not empty the full engine's own page cache (F-BROW-79, 2026-11-13).** In the built-in reader a page kept in the cache is asked for again after a rule changed; the full engine keeps its own cache, so a page it already holds is reloaded by the person (Ctrl+R) to carry the new header.
* KI-710 **PDFs protected with AES-256 (revisions 5 and 6) cannot be opened (F-BROX-07, 2026-11-13).** RC4 (40 to 128 bit) and AES-128 files open with their password; AES-256 needs SHA-384 and SHA-512, which the reader does not have, so it says so and stops. Print and copy limits in a protected file are not enforced or shown.
* KI-711 **The decoded-picture and parsed-style caches and Happy Eyeballs are tested but not measured (F-BROX-11, 13, 14, 2026-11-13).** They are in the browser and the resolver; how much time they save on a real page and a real network with a dead IPv6 route has not been measured.
* KI-712 **HTTP/2 is connected but not measured and has limits (F-BROX-10, 2026-11-13).** The browser's HTTP client now offers `h2` (ALPN), keeps one connection per host and falls back to HTTP/1.1; it was checked against a server of our own and, by hand, against three real sites. Not done: server push, HTTP/2 through a proxy, keep-alive PINGs on idle connections, sharing a connection between hosts with one certificate. Nobody has measured whether pages load faster.
* KI-713 **Safe browsing is built but only reachable by chat (F-BROW-98, 2026-11-13).** Mixed content is blocked for scripts, style sheets and pictures; the known-bad site list and the certificate details view exist as chat tools and an explanation page, with a Settings row for the certificate view (Settings -> Browser, key K) but no Settings page for the known-bad list and no lock-icon view in the browser window. The certificate view does its own handshake (it is not the certificate the open tab used). No list ships; the suggested URLhaus list was not downloaded in a test and its licence is quoted from the publisher. Only whole sites are listed, not paths.
* KI-714 **SVG drawing still leaves out some parts (F-BROX-05, 2026-11-13).** Patterns, markers, nested `svg` view boxes and the common filter primitives are drawn. Not drawn: an `image` inside an SVG, `feTurbulence`, `feMorphology`, `feConvolveMatrix` and the lighting filters, `href` inheritance between patterns, the clipping of markers and nested `svg` to their box, markers set by a style sheet rule (only the attribute or `style` on the shape works), the clipping of a `symbol` to its viewport, the sub-region of one filter primitive (the filter region is used for all of them) and a `color-interpolation-filters` set on one primitive instead of the filter. Q-A 2026-11-14: the filter region (default and `filterUnits`), linearRGB as the default filter colour space and the viewBox of a `symbol` used with a size are built, found by comparing 21 drawings with a real headless Chrome (the comparison skips where there is no Chrome); text is not compared (the fonts differ).
* KI-715 **PDF forms: only text, choice and check box fields can be filled in (F-BROX-08, 2026-11-13).** Radio buttons, push buttons and signatures are refused; a multi-line field breaks lines only where the person typed a line break (no wrapping in the new appearance); comb fields, JavaScript formatting and calculations are not run; the new appearance uses Helvetica; protected files (RC4, AES) are not filled; the copy is always a new file (the original is never changed). Opened in Apple PDFKit the filled files show the values; other readers were not tried.
* KI-716 **Clicks and typing on a scripted page in the reader work for buttons, check boxes and text fields only (F-BJS-01, 2026-11-13, narrowed in round 4).** A button, a check box, a radio button and text typed into a field or a text area reach the page's scripts and the page is drawn again; links and other elements with handlers, a submit button inside a form (the reader submits it), passwords, key events and timers that run while the page is looked at do not. Video and canvas stay with the full engine.

## Assistant, agents and services

* KI-023 **Live checks:** Ollama (earlier) and a Lemonade server on the LAN (Mac mini, 2026-10-18/19). OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini and OpenRouter are
  implemented against their documentation and tested with scripted servers; no live verification without keys. (F-AI-02) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (F-AI-02 is planned: only Ollama and the Lemonade server were used live).
* KI-099 **Live-model findings (qwen3-8b on a Lemonade server on the LAN, 2026-11-12):** (a) once in three attempts the model answered in prose that a row "is
  already marked done" without calling `board_update`. Fixed: a reply that names a board row as done while the board says otherwise now gets one correction
  line appended (`boardtools::false_done_claims`, scripted test); the board is never edited by it. The false claim did not recur in the three live
  re-runs, so the live proof is only that nothing broke. (b) The planning rule ("one planned row per step") was followed with a single lumped row in two of
  three Autopilot attempts; the work and the board were still correct. (c) Fixed: the wrong-model sentence said "not available for this account"; a LAN
  server now gets "has no model with that name", and "No model loaded: x" gets "has not loaded that model right now". (d) Each request of a workspace chat
  carries about 5,300 prompt tokens before any history; with a busy shared server a request takes 5 to 15 seconds, and two runs that ask for different
  models at once make the server unload one ("No model loaded"). (F-ASSIST-20, F-ASSIST-15, F-CHAT-24, F-ASSIST-23)
* KI-100 **The k3s build-and-deploy skill did not always make an 8B model write a complete manifest.** The first live run (0 of 3) was partly the check's own
  fake: its `k8s_apply` took a `manifest` text, the real tool takes `manifest_path` or `yaml`. With the real arguments the old skill gave 2 of 3 (one manifest
  lacked limits and non-root). The skill now carries a manifest template to copy (probes, requests and limits, runAsNonRoot): 3 of 3 attempts, qwen3-8b. With
  other models the check gave qwen3-coder-30b 1 of 3 (step limit of 14 turns) and qwen2.5-coder-14b 0 of 3 (it did not run the build, load, apply, status order).
  (F-IDE-19)
* KI-101 **What the live runs did not cover:** F-IDE-18 (a screen row: input, Send and the Model/Agent/Approvals row at five sizes) has nothing a model can
  prove and was not run; F-IDE-15 was judged from `.iyakki/board-assignments.json`, not from the Board screen; F-FLOW-29 used a fake camera that returns
  a solid red picture, no real camera; F-FLOW-34's watch-and-rollback after a publish and the daily caps were not exercised; F-AI-02 covers only the
  Lemonade (OpenAI-compatible) path, OpenAI, Claude and Gemini were NOT tried. The evidence (redacted request summaries, timings, token counts per attempt)
  is kept outside the repository.
* KI-110 **WASM tiles in the shell are experimental (F-WASM-09, F-APPS-45, 2026-11-12):** a tile app with a `.wasm` module runs only while Settings -> Developer mode -> WASM tiles is on (off by default). The shell paints the module with the tile host's reference renderer (rectangles, outlines, a small built-in font, not the shell's own fonts), the module gets no service calls (no tools, no files, no network), and the web page of the app is not shown. It was tried on macOS in the headless shell test only, not from a signed package, on a Linux machine or on real hardware; no WASM tile is in the built-in catalog. A `.svg` icon of a tile app still shows the first letter on the launcher (only `.png` icons are drawn).
* KI-107 **Fixed in code 2026-11-12: the in-house xz reader did not check the index or the stream footer.** `iyakki-archive::xz::decompress` verified the stream
  header, every block header and every block's check, but skipped the index and the 12-byte footer. It now compares the index with the blocks it decoded (record
  count, unpadded and uncompressed size of each), the zero padding, the index CRC32, and the footer (CRC32, backward size, stream flags equal to the header's, the
  magic `YZ`); a block's optional sizes in its header and its zero padding are checked too. Found by the fuzz test (2026-11-12); regression
  `fuzz_xz_regression_index_and_footer_are_verified` flips a bit in every byte of the index and footer for all four check types.
* KI-108 **Fixed in code 2026-11-12: `Tree::release_nodes` was not idempotent.** Releasing the same detached subtree twice put its node numbers on the free list
  twice, so two new nodes would have shared one number (`iyakki-web::domtree`). A set of the free numbers now makes a second release a no-op and
  `detached_subtree` finds nothing at a released number. (A release that comes after the numbers were handed out again cannot be told from a first release; the
  one caller, `DomModule::reclaim`, is guarded by the set of nodes a script holds.) Regression `fuzz_node_arena_regression_a_second_release_of_a_subtree_is_a_no_op`,
  and the random arena test now releases every subtree twice.
* KI-109 **Fixed in code 2026-11-12: a hostile ssh-agent could keep a sign-in waiting.** The agent client (`iyakki-link::sshagent`) allowed 10 seconds per read, so a
  slow drip could hold it for very long, and accepted a frame of 256 KB. The whole exchange now has 5 seconds, and a signature answer over 16 KB (a key list over
  64 KB) is refused. Regressions with a silent agent, a one-byte-a-second agent and oversize frames: `fuzz_sshagent_regression_*`.
* KI-024 **Claude Code** (native installer and sign-in) and **GitHub Copilot CLI** (installer and sign-in) were installed and signed in on the Mac mini.
  **Copilot with full tool use is not verified:** bridged mode fixed the handshake bug, but a run with real tool calls and a real sign-in end
  to end has not been done. **Autopilot with external agents** needs 'Allow Auto' in Settings, Agents and was not run end to end. Codex and
  Gemini CLI adapters were tested only against fake CLIs. (docs/EXTERNAL-AGENTS.md) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true; F-JOBS-04 is partial 100/30.
* KI-025 **Claude Code's own foreground Bash cannot be switched off by IYAKKI.** The generated instructions tell it to call `mcp__iyakki__job_start` for
  slow work, but the vendor's built-in shell is outside IYAKKI's policy bridge. Common long commands (cargo build/test, npm install, docker build, make, ...) are
  now refused with `--disallowedTools`, but the shell as a whole stays on, and the settings hook that would point to `job_start` is not built because the
  vendor setting names are unconfirmed. Not tried against a real Claude Code. (F-JOBS-04) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-026 **Developer mode (`iyakki dev ...`)** was first tested on loopback only. Since 2026-10-26 it has been used against real computers over the network (the Mac mini 2018 and the MacBook Pro through `iyakki dev`, which is how KI-090 to KI-095 were found with `dist/hwtest/hwcheck.py`); the shell grant, files, updates and state routes were exercised there. Not tried: every scope on every machine, and the new terminal (`dev shell --tty`, F-DEV-16) on a device. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** reworded above.
* KI-027 **Tile-app one-step publishing (`tile_ship`)** was tested on loopback only, not against a store deployed on k3s. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-028 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** **Private store in the cluster:** the one-click in-cluster registry has tests with fakes only and was not run on the real cluster. It is open
  to the network on NodePort 30500 with no password. The store server itself ran over loopback and once in a local container.
  Fixed in the tree 2026-10-28 (the registry now listens on 127.0.0.1 only): `localreg::tests::the_registry_manifest_has_no_network_exposure`, `localreg::tests::an_existing_nodeport_registry_is_converted_to_loopback_and_audited`, `localreg::tests::a_cluster_on_another_machine_is_refused_because_the_registry_listens_on_loopback_only`; verify on the image: `curl http://<ip>:30500/` must fail from another computer. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the exposure is fixed in the tree (tests above); the rest (a real cluster) is still true. **Hardware/QEMU check:** same as KI-004, `curl http://<ip>:30500/` from another computer must fail.
* KI-029 **Platform services (Gitea, the private store)**, jobs, voice engines and TALES pictures: fakes and scripted stand-ins; never a real cluster,
  speaker or picture model. A real k3s ran on the Mac mini, but these services were not deployed to it. `docs/PLATFORM-SERVICES.md`,
  `docs/JOBS.md`, `docs/VOICE.md` and `docs/STORE.md` end with what was and was not verified. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-030 **Dashboards:** no real Prometheus/Elasticsearch/ClickHouse server of each kind was used in tests. DuckDB (Python wheel) and Elasticsearch
  were installed by Quick install on the Mac mini; the dashboard connectors were not driven against them. (docs/DASHBOARD.md) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-031 **Picture generation:** Ollama's image models run only on macOS.
  none is shipped. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-032 **Voice engines are not shipped** (GPL or voice licences of their own); IYAKKI finds what the computer has, or the browser's voice. (F-VOICE-05..07) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-033 **TALES picture quality with real models is unjudged.** Small local models mostly cannot draw rich SVG, which is why the built-in cartoon
  composer exists. (F-TALES-06, 13, 15..18) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-034 **Withdrawing (yanking) a package from a private store does not disable copies already installed.** They are now marked "Withdrawn" (STORE tile, `store list`)
  and removal is offered, but they keep running until removed; only a root-signed revocation switches them off. (F-APPS-40, docs/STORE.md section 13) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (F-APPS-40 is done for the server side; a revoked copy keeps running until removed).
* KI-035 **Approvals:** session grants are for the chat and the IDE chat only; INBOX approvals and Parallel-tasks workers ask every time. (F-ASSIST-40) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (F-ASSIST-40 is done as described).
* KI-036 **Audit-log chain:** the race is fixed for one process only; two processes on one `audit.log` can still race, and a chain that already reads
  BROKEN stays BROKEN (start a new log; docs/ADMIN-GUIDE.md). (F-SHELL-01)
  Fixed in the tree 2026-10-28 for the race (an exclusive flock around read-last-hash, append and fsync): `accounts::tests::two_processes_and_two_threads_appending_at_once_keep_one_unbroken_chain`, `accounts::tests::a_line_cut_short_by_a_power_loss_does_not_break_the_next_append_under_the_lock`.
  A BROKEN chain still stays BROKEN (the code never rewrites it), but there is now a command to start a new log: USERS, audit log, capital `N` moves the old file aside as `audit.log.broken-<time>` and begins a new chain with an `audit.restart` line (`accounts::tests::a_broken_chain_is_kept_aside_and_a_new_log_starts_that_names_it`, `users::tests::n_in_the_audit_view_starts_a_new_log_only_when_the_chain_is_broken_and_keeps_the_old_file`). Verified by those tests on the development computer only, not on a device. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true as written: fixed for one machine, tested on the development computer only.
* KI-037 **Several stores and the portal:** the official address is empty ("coming soon") and up to 8 stores can be added. (F-APPSTORE-18, 19) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: `OFFICIAL_STORE_URL` is empty in `store/catalog.rs`; F-APPSTORE-19 is partial 100/30 and never ran against a real portal.
* KI-038 **Message box limits:** 20,000 characters in chat, 4000 in the TALES idea box; longer text is cut with a visible notice. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-039 Many assistant helpers are heuristics with **English-only phrase tables** (refusal retry, stall guard, advice detection, tool-call repair,
  credential guard). Do not paste secrets into the chat; use the vault. Most assistant machinery was driven with scripted models; only some of
  it with a live qwen-class model. "Done needs proof" can be bypassed by editing the board file by hand. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-460 **Google Gemini (experimental, optional, off by default):** audio and text go to Google only after you answer Yes on the consent card (docs/GEMINI-PROVIDER.md). On the free tier Google may use what is sent to improve its products and people may review it, according to Google's published terms (check them; they can change); a paid key is not used for training by those terms. The free tier has small limits: after "too many requests" IYAKKI waits as Google asks (bounded) and then says the limit is reached. Nothing was tried against Google: the tests use a fake transport, so the request shape and the OpenAI-compatible chat endpoint are as documented by Google, not verified; a live test exists but was not run. A recording over 15 MB is refused, not split. (F-VOICE-41, F-ASSIST-52)

## Accounts, files and security

* KI-040 **IYAKKI's own accounts only.** IYAKKI accounts are not Linux users; **user files are root-owned** on the data volume (`/home`). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true; KI-095 (fixed in code, pending) changes who can read the home folders, not who owns them.
* KI-041 **Firewall (`docs/FIREWALL.md`).** New incoming connections are blocked by default, outgoing ones are not filtered, forwarding is not touched. Host-name rules are only as trustworthy as the DNS server. The rules and the boot order were tested with fakes and a real kernel in a network namespace, not on a device or together with a running k3s; a machine whose kernel lacks nf_tables says so and is not protected. The firewall page is in Settings (no System tile). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-042 **The root filesystem is in RAM.** Changes to the system outside `/var/lib/iyakki` and `/home` vanish at reboot. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true. The layered root (base, core and ui layers, F-BOOT-41) keeps its layer files on the boot or data volume, but the writable layer above them is still RAM-backed (`docs/development/LAYERED-ROOT-DESIGN.md`), so nothing outside `/var/lib/iyakki` and `/home` persists.
* KI-043 The data volume is not encrypted. The browser front end has a self-signed certificate and has had no outside review. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true for the volume (F-STORE-05 is partial 60/30); an encrypted home folder is available per account (see KI-001).
* KI-044 GitHub Copilot CLI's sign-in token moved into the vault in a later build than the one first tested on the Mac mini. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true as history.

* KI-102 **UPnP/DLNA media server, one part left (security review 2026-11-12, low):** it listens on every interface and answers any private or link-local source, so a VPN or container network counts as local. Closed the same day: a request that does not arrive whole within 10 s is dropped (`ServeCfg::head_deadline`); the file is opened without following a link and must be the same file (device and inode) the shared folder's path leads to; `ssdp:all` gets one answer (the root device) and the connection manager is not answered on its own. Not done: binding only to the private interfaces, and the `MX` delay. Detail: `docs/development/SECURITY-REVIEW-2026-11-12.md`.
* KI-103 **Developer-mode terminal audit (security review 2026-11-12, closed in the tree):** the lines typed into an interactive terminal are not recorded, by decision: the end-of-session audit line now says how many bytes were typed and in how many lines (`typed N bytes in M lines (not recorded)`). Neither the keys nor a hash of them are kept, because a typed password would be open to guessing from a hash. The plain (non-terminal) mode still logs every line.
* KI-104 **Signing with an ssh-agent key (security review 2026-11-12, low):** `DeviceKey::sign_bytes` hands any message to the agent. Every caller today signs the `iyakki-vault-v2` text, which cannot be an SSH login or SSHSIG signature, but nothing in the code refuses another message; a future caller that signs text chosen by a remote party would make the agent a signing oracle for the person's SSH key.
* KI-105 **Backups (security review 2026-11-12, low):** in a file that is not encrypted, the account name is only a claim in the file, so "made for this account" proves nothing; a file placed in the home folder can name the person's account and carry skills or settings. The card of `backup_restore` names the file and the areas and always asks (also in Auto since this review), but the content is not checked beyond its checksums. A passphrase is held in an ordinary string while it is used.
* KI-106 **Replay edges (security review 2026-11-12, low):** the signed-request text joins the query as `k=v&k=v` without escaping, so two different queries can have the same text (a third party cannot use this without breaking the TLS pin and the key); the nonce memory drops the oldest nonce after 20,000 signed requests inside the two-minute window. Closed: payment `ref`s are now kept for the last 7 days they were used and at most 5000 (was the last 50).
* KI-130 **Web pages refreshed by the events stream (F-WEB-16, M20):** the JavaScript changes (`app.js` `poll(..., {push: true})`, `bell.js` `live()` and the `changed` event, `cloud.js`, `remote.js`) were written on a Mac with no `node` and no Chrome and have never been executed; the node tests and `dist/browsertest/run_pages.py` were not repeated. The server side is tested with a real Rust WebSocket client over TLS.
* KI-131 **Programs listed under a person (F-SYS-01, M20):** only the assistant's `run_command` claims the programs it starts (`procowner`). Terminals in the CLI tile, scripts the editor runs in the cluster and store installs are not claimed, so they do not appear on the Mine page; `procowner::claim` is the call for them.
* KI-132 **Network use per process (F-SYS-01, M20):** a host process shows connections, listening ports and UDP sockets only; received and sent bytes exist only for a process in a network namespace of its own (a container), because the kernel counts bytes per namespace. Per-process bytes for host processes would need eBPF or conntrack accounting and are not built.
* KI-133 **Editor run input (F-EDIT-16, M20):** a script run with input (`IYAKKI_STDIN` through a `sh` wrapper) and the real stop were tested only against the stand-in cluster, not a real k3s; the Input bar was not looked at on a screen.
* KI-150 **Download list (F-QA-11, M22):** a fetched list now changes the version of thirteen tools, but the download address and the checksum address of every tool still come from the program's own templates, nothing is published at the address yet (so no real fetch was ever made), and `cli_download_list_check` was not run in a test because it reaches the network. The release gate's new manifest step does not check the signature.
* KI-151 **Add source form fills late (F-DASH-12, M22):** the address of an open Add source form is filled when a slow cluster finally answers; tested with a slow fake cluster on a Mac, not looked at on a screen and not against a real k3s.
* KI-152 **Tenant secrets need a pinned vault (F-CLOUD-33, M22):** the tenant configuration's secrets are written, read and removed only when `IYAKKI_VAULT_FINGERPRINT` is set; without it the page shows the sentence and the vault still lists names. Tested against a loopback vault service only, not in a real pod on a cluster.

## Not built yet (stubs and plans)

## SSH tunnels

* **Not tried against a real SSH host.** Everything is tested with a fake client (docs/SSH-TUNNELS.md, "What is verified"). That OpenSSH 10.2 accepts the exact arguments and that its error texts match the mapping is unproven.
* **`-J` (jump hosts) may not work on the images.** OpenSSH starts the jump hop with the `ssh` of its compiled-in path (`/usr/bin/ssh`), which on an image is Dropbear's client; the git-ssh package installs OpenSSH only as `/usr/libexec/iyakki/git-ssh`. A tunnel with a jump chain may fail there until the package sets `_PATH_SSH_PROGRAM`. A single host is not affected.
* **Per-site routing is not built**: the Browser does not choose a tunnel by domain yet (seam: `sshtunnel::SiteRouter`). Open the address shown on the page.
* A host that git also uses loses its trusted-host line when the last tunnel using it is deleted; git then shows the fingerprint again.
* IPv6 literal host names are not accepted in a tunnel; use a name.

## Install and storage

* KI-053 Two installers, both erase the whole drive you pick: `iyakki-usb` (writes the stick from another computer; `--all` for internal disks) and
  `iyakki install` / SETTINGS, System, Install to disk (docs/ZRAM-AND-INSTALL.md). `iyakki-usb` wrote and verified the stick for the Mac mini 2018
  (GPT). The on-device installer ran only in QEMU against virtual disks; 4096-byte-sector drives were not tried. The first internal-disk install of a T2 MacBook booted on
  2026-10-24 (owner); every other real internal disk, and a second boot, an upgrade and a power cut on it, are untested (F-INST-03). Not done: "install next to another system", an encrypted data volume, an installer on the aarch64 image (F-INST-04). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true. Component updates with boot confirmation were tried on the Mac mini 2018 (alpha.2 notes), which covers one upgrade path on one machine; the installer itself is still QEMU only.
* KI-054 **DISKS tile** (mount, unmount, eject, partition manager, [docs/DISKS.md](docs/DISKS.md)): written and tested against a made-up machine only (fake `/sys`, `/proc` and mount runner); it has never mounted, ejected or partitioned a real drive. Try the partition manager on a spare USB stick only. The images' kernels lack the NTFS, exFAT, Btrfs, XFS and HFS+ drivers, so those drives are listed but not opened; BitLocker and LUKS cannot be unlocked; exFAT and NTFS formatting and resizing are not supported (F-DISKS-08). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true for the Linux tile on real drives.
* KI-055 **zram** (compressed swap) was seen in QEMU only; behaviour under real memory pressure is untested. There is no hibernation. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-056 The release signing key and the download page were new at alpha.1; `SHA256SUMS` and the signed `release.json` come with every release since (RELEASE-NOTES-0.1.0-alpha.2.md). Compare the printed public key with the one in
  the README. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** reworded above.
* KI-057 The images in a release are built by `./run.sh build`; an image made before the version bump says `unknown` for the version in About. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.

  flow 187; core and remote above 200). Not yet one green command: the tests the other work in progress had broken are listed there. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** no single current number is claimed. Some tests are skipped on macOS on purpose (`ignore = "..."`), and the K8S tests of the IDE crate were seen running over a minute each on a Mac, probably waiting on a missing `kubectl`. **Check for the parent:** one `cargo test --workspace` on Linux with a clean `TMPDIR` and write the totals here.

Leaves this file when `./run.sh test` has been seen to pass on a quiet machine. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true as written (fixed in the tree; the entry leaves the file when `./run.sh test` has been seen to pass on a quiet machine).

## Licence notes

* KI-065 The images contain GPL/LGPL programs as separate executables (Linux, BusyBox, GRUB, ...); whoever redistributes an image must offer their
  source (`legal-info/`). The libre image has no proprietary firmware. Third-party tools (`ffmpeg`, Claude Code, Copilot CLI and others) are
  downloaded from the vendor at the user's request and are never shipped.
  See `docs/LICENSES.md`. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-066 **Restricted firmware is not in the images** (2026-10-23, [docs/RESTRICTED-FIRMWARE.md](docs/RESTRICTED-FIRMWARE.md)): Qualcomm Bluetooth patch files under `LICENSE.qcom`, PCIe Realtek `rtl_nic`, Broadcom `tigon`/`bnx2`/`bnx2x` and `carl9170-1.fw` are installed by the person at a click (Store, Setup, Hardware) or built into their own image. Until then: the Qualcomm laptop Bluetooth chips do not work, bnx2/bnx2x server NICs and AR9170 USB Wi-Fi sticks do not start, tg3 and r8169 ports work at reduced function. All of it is tested with fakes only: no image was built and nothing was tried on a device; the PCI/USB id tables may miss a board (the folder route and `--force-hardware` cover that). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-067 **The shell crate's tests needed more than 8 GB in one process** (found 2026-10-28; **fixed 2026-10-28**). Measured cause: not live shells or app threads (a counting allocator showed under 150 MB in use at any time while the process held 1.5 GB after 600 tests) but glibc: each test runs on its own thread, every thread gets a malloc arena, and the arenas keep the pages the test freed, so about 3.5 MB per test stayed resident. Fix: `crates/iyakki-shell/src/testmem.rs` (tests only, Linux glibc; started from `Shell::new` and the `temp_dir` test helper) fixes the mmap threshold at 128 KB so big buffers go back when freed, and a helper thread calls `malloc_trim(0)` twice a second. The whole lib run (`RUST_TEST_THREADS=4`, 8 GB cap, no swap) now peaks at 2.2 GB resident (about 50 minutes) and finishes: 2,503 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored. Known remainder: tests that log in leave two threads each (`flow-tick`, `flow-assist`, started by the login for the schedule runner) running until the process ends (about 850 threads at the end, small); stopping them on `Shell` drop would need a stop signal in `iyakki-agent` flows (not done). The tests that start the fake web helper build it on first use; run them from the cargo-built test binary, not from a copy. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: fixed on 2026-10-28 for Linux glibc; the mitigation is tests-only and not used on macOS.
* KI-069 **Browser microphone: a page's `getUserMedia` never answers on the Mac, and the cause is not proven** (found 2026-10-28; **open**). Seen on a plain test page served from k3s (`http://127.0.0.1:<nodeport>/`, secure origin) with `audio: true` and with explicit flags, and on three recorder sites; the first test on one site did record. Evidence: the mic plugin opens `plughw:1,0` each time; the engine log (diagnosis switch `/tmp/iyakki-dev/gst-debug`) shows the capturer start, `audiornnoise` missing (a warning), then 3 ms later an audio output on `autoaudiosink` that fails (`Could not open device for playback in stereo mode`, `sink not negotiated`) and then nothing for 30 s. Working theory: the web process waits synchronously for that output and never answers. Not proven: why the output fails (the developer shell runs as an unprivileged user and sees no sound cards), and why capture starts an output at all. Next: keep the engine's alsasink warnings in the kept log, add a pass/fail "audio output" button to the test page, read the plugin's delivered-buffer count. **Update 2026-10-28 (image 1028k, thread states and ALSA status read on the Mac):** the microphone itself works (the EMEET capture substream is RUNNING); the page then starts the engine's WebAudio output on its own thread and sets it to PLAYING synchronously, and that output cannot negotiate (`Could not open device for playback in stereo mode`, `webaudioSrc not-negotiated`), so the page freezes (the Mac does not). Cause found in the code: the T2 plan moved from `Direct` (volume over hw plus the engine's `iyakkiweb` PCM) to `Soft` (over plughw) and so lost the engine PCM; `plug > softvol > plughw` was measured earlier not to take what WebAudio asks of this device. Trials on the Mac with hot patches of `/etc/asound.conf` (a patch file without IYAKKI's header line is left alone by the sound watcher): the engine PCM `iyakkiweb` as `null` or as `plug > softvol > hw` (`plugweb`) ends the freeze; `linear > route` (image 1028l) did not; with `plugweb` a media element's tone is heard. Fix (next image, **not yet confirmed on the Mac as an image**): `Plan::SoftWeb` keeps plughw for the players and writes `iyakkiweb` as `plug` over a second softvol on `hw` (same slider: one control, two softvols). Test-page notes: a WebAudio context starts `suspended` until the page calls `resume()` (autoplay is denied where the output cannot be shared), and each new AudioContext holds the T2 speaker, so a page that never closes its contexts gets `busy`. Still open: the engine blocking its page when any output fails. KI-072: MediaRecorder needs `encodebin` and `uritranscodebin` (WebKit's recorder pipeline); both were missing, now built (`BR2_PACKAGE_GST1_PLUGINS_BASE_PLUGIN_ENCODING`, gst-plugins-bad `-Dtranscode=enabled`). **Fixed in the next image (1028n), confirmed on the Mac with hot patches 2026-10-28:** (1) the engine's sound output on a T2 is `iyakkiwebaudiosink` (`dist/webview/wpe-helper/websink.c`): audioconvert, audioresample, then ALSA asked the way `aplay` asks (`snd_pcm_set_params`), and silence when the device cannot be used, so a page never freezes on its output; the helper picks it where `/etc/asound.conf` names `iyakkiweb` (`IYAKKI_WEBVIEW_AUDIOSINK=0` keeps the old output). The old `alsasink` failed `snd_pcm_hw_params_set_channels` with -22 through every chain tried, and the page froze. (2) MediaRecorder: `libgstencoding.so` and `libgsttranscode.so` added (boot self-test `path web-recorder`). Open: after a write error (`snd_pcm_writei` -> I/O error seen once on the T2 speaker) the stream stays silent until its next start; the microphone and the speaker are single-use devices, so two engines (the Browser tile, the AI IDE's browser tab) block each other (microphone through `dsnoop` and the speaker through the IYAKKI mixer are the planned sharing). **Later, confirmed on the Mac (2026-10-28):** WebAudio played one scratchy tone and then nothing, because the engine starts a new output pipeline whenever a page resumes its audio or starts a media element while the previous one still holds the single-use T2 speaker (every later open failed `Resource busy`). The sink plugin now keeps ONE shared device handle and ONE mixer thread per engine process: every sink is a voice with its own ring buffer, the thread adds the voices and writes 20 ms pieces, closes the device after 1.5 s without a voice, reopens after a write error, and retries a busy device; Beep (WebAudio) and the audio-element tone both play. Still true: two engine processes (the Browser tile and the AI IDE's browser) exclude each other, and the IYAKKI players share the device only through the mixer route.
* KI-070 **MP3, AAC and H.264 in the browser** (2026-10-28). MP3 now decodes in the engine through `iyakkimp3dec` (minimp3, CC0; image `1028j`, boot self-test `path mp3-decoder`; **not yet confirmed on the Mac**). AAC and H.264 need the add-on, which the administrator supplies (owner decision 2026-11-04: IYAKKI ships and hosts no FFmpeg, not even the archive). The image carries the parked bridge plugin and the recipe (`/usr/share/iyakki/av-addon/`); a package in `/local/packages`, a mirror, or an installed ffmpeg's own shared libraries (when they pass the checks) is installed after the administrator reads its NOTICE and confirms. In QEMU an H.264+AAC page plays with the add-on and shows the bar without it (docs/development/AV-ADDON-BUILD.md). **Still open:** the images carry no bridge plugin until `./build.sh web` is run again (a repack cannot add it), the Settings -> Browser add-on page for the mirror is not built, the `media.codecs` tenant switch is only documented, and nothing is confirmed on the Mac. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true; F-BROW-72 (the recorder pipeline) is partial 100/30 and Buildroot was not run with the switch.
* KI-071 **The IDE assistant applied an image it never built** (2026-10-28). With neither podman nor docker on the image, `deploy_build` fails, and the assistant went on to apply `mic-recorder:latest` (ImagePullBackOff). The skills `k3s-node-webapp` and `k3s-web-service-patterns` now carry a no-builder rule (stop; for a small app use a stock pinned image with the files in a ConfigMap); whether to ship podman in the Mac and web images is an owner decision (size not measured). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (an owner decision on podman is not recorded).
* KI-072 **Sign-in and the vault** (owner decision 2026-10-28: no automatic sign-in). The Mac was seen locked out of its vault after boot ("vault is locked", the Gitea hand-off gave up after 40 tries). The tree has no code path that signs anyone in without the password (the sign-in screen only remembers the NAME; the browser sign-in checks the password first), so the cause of that Mac observation is **not proven** and the machine was not touched. What is built and tested on the development computer: tests that the sign-in screen stays put with a remembered name, that an empty or wrong password signs nobody in and the right one signs in and opens the vault (`shell::nosignin_tests`), and a scan of the program and the image scripts for any other sign-in route or auto-login switch; a Quick install hand-off that finds the vault locked now waits at once instead of spending 40 tries, writes the plain sentence ("... password is waiting for your vault: sign in with your password or open Settings -> Vault -> Unlock") to the job log, audit, inbox and the Store card, and finishes by itself when the vault opens (`vaultpending::tests::a_hand_off_with_a_locked_vault_waits_at_once_...`); Settings -> Vault is the one place that says LOCKED and names what waits (`settings::vaultstate_tests`). **Not verified on the Mac**: sign in with the password there, install Gitea, confirm no "locked" appears; if it still does, `iyakki dev diag` is needed to find out how the vault stayed shut. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true: `F-SHELL-01` is done (no automatic sign-in), and the Mac observation of a locked vault remains unproven. **Hardware check:** on the Mac mini sign in with the password, install Gitea from Store -> Setup, and confirm no "locked" appears; if it does, run `iyakki dev diag`.
* KI-073 **Sleep on Apple T2 MacBooks wakes badly** (tried on the Mac 2026-10-30, image 1028u, kernel with suspend). Sleep does start and the machine does wake, but the wake takes a long time, **Wi-Fi did not come back**, and the **keyboard did not work at the sign-in screen** (the session is locked before sleeping, so a password could not be typed). This is the known Linux problem with the T2 chip, the apple-bce driver (keyboard) and the Wi-Fi card not resuming. Not fixed in this release: treat Sleep as experimental on T2 Macs, keep your work saved, and hold the power button if the keyboard does not return. The Sleep button appears only when the kernel lists a sleep state. Next steps: unload and reload apple-bce and the Wi-Fi driver around suspend (a suspend hook), read the kernel log from a wake, and consider hiding Sleep on T2 hardware until it resumes cleanly. (F-KBD-16) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true. Note for F-KBD-16: its note says "the kernel with suspend is not built yet" while this entry records a try on image 1028u with a suspend kernel; one of the two is out of date. **Hardware check:** suspend and wake a T2 MacBook Pro with the current image and write down the wake time, Wi-Fi and the keyboard at the sign-in screen.
* KI-074 **LEARN lessons are unproven with a real model** (2026-10-29). The lesson of a day (F-STUDY-05, F-STUDY-06) was built and tested only with scripted models: no real model has written a lesson, answered a question or drawn a picture through it, so the quality of the text, how well a small model follows the five-section format, and whether the SVG drawings are any good are all unknown (a drawing the sanitiser refuses becomes a plain drawn placeholder). A picture model's own pictures (the raster path) were not run at all. Read aloud was tested with a fake speaker, not on a real one, and the page was not looked at on a real screen or a phone. A lesson is not in the backup. The tile uses the chat model, not TALES' own model choice. On the Mac (2026-10-30) the owner opened plans and lessons and accepted them for this release; picture quality and small-model behaviour are still open. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (F-STUDY-05 and F-STUDY-06 are scripted-model tests only).
* KI-075 **Linux windows install still fails on the Mac** (tried 2026-10-30, image 1028u, after the initramfs fix of the sandbox entry). The Debian download and unpack work; the apt step still fails, and the reason was not captured before the Mac stopped answering. The fix for `pivot_root` is proven in QEMU only; a full apt run inside the sandbox on an image has never completed. Read the Installs row's Log on the Mac for the reason, then fix. Not fixed in this release. (F-LINAPP-03) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true. Note for F-LINAPP-03: its note says "not yet run on the Mac"; this entry says it was and the install failed. **Hardware check:** on the Mac mini read the Installs row's Log for the apt failure, or run `iyakki linuxapp-init` by hand through `iyakki dev shell`.
* KI-076 **VPN is untried on hardware** (kernel with WireGuard built, image 1028t onward). The WireGuard module is in the image; no tunnel was brought up against a real server, and the OpenVPN download and the helper were not exercised. Shipped as untested. (F-VPN-11 to F-VPN-16) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true (F-VPN-11 to F-VPN-16 are partial at 30 to 50 on test). **Hardware check:** bring a WireGuard tunnel up against a real server from the VPN page and read the kill-switch line on the Firewall page.
* KI-068 **Download versions and checksums still change only with a new ISO** (2026-10-28). The signed downloads manifest ([docs/development/DOWNLOADS-MANIFEST.md](docs/development/DOWNLOADS-MANIFEST.md), F-QA-11) has its safe core (format, rules, signature, rollback protection, last-good copy, `./run.sh dlmanifest`) and the version lookups of kubectl and helm are wired to it. The fetch is built (F-QA-12: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/meisync/iyakki-downloads/main/downloads.json`, once per 24 h and on "Check for updates" on the CLIs tab), but it was only tested against a scripted network: the repository may not exist yet (a 404 is a quiet "no update"), the real signed file was never fetched, and nothing ran on the Mac. Every other item still uses the constants in the code. The 35 REVIEW rows are unchanged by this. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true; the downloads repository is not recorded as existing.
* KI-077 **Closed for taps and drags; gestures with several fingers open.** **USB-C touch monitor on the Mac mini: touch is dead after a boot with the cable connected** (measured 2026-11-03, monitor with panel
  27c0:0859 behind hub 1a40:0101). The panel binds to `hid-multitouch` at 3.6 s, the hub drops at about 4.7 s when the display is taken over and
  comes back, and no finger event arrives until the USB-C cable is pulled and put back. Not enough: switching the panel or its hub off and on in
  software (Settings → Keyboard → Probe touch screen, both steps), the monitor's power button, `i915.fastboot=1` (the driver has no such parameter).
  What worked: plugging the USB-C cable in while the system is running, and (reported by the owner, not logged) the display probe that runs with a
  monitor already connected, which was removed once and is back; the boot option `iyakki.touchscreen=probe|off|auto` (menu: More options, "touch
  screen") sets it. A cable from the monitor's separate USB port was not seen by the Mac at all. Unverified: whether the restored probe wakes the
  panel on a cold boot (needs a new image).
  Evidence 2026-11-04 (device log `dmesg-1104c`, GRUB, cold boot): both HID interfaces are opened at 3.7 s but no finger event arrives for 100+ s.
  The hub (usb 1-5 and 1-5.3) disconnects at 4.546 s, re-enumerates at 5.2-5.9 s and again at 11.3 s (our one-time re-attach), and stays silent;
  replugging the cable fixes it. Timeline of our code: splash stops 4.459 s, USB disconnect 4.546 s, `shell: display:` line 4.603 s, probe 4.925 s.
  So the drop comes BEFORE the shell's display setup and before the probe; the splash's whole-frame pan (FBIOPAN_DISPLAY at 3.757 s, same
  offsets, 0.8 s earlier) did not bounce USB. Our calls that could touch the mode (all audited): `KDSETMODE 1` (splash at 3.57 s and shell, kernel
  treats the same mode as a no-op; now read with KDGETMODE first and logged), `FBIOPAN_DISPLAY` with the current offsets in `Framebuffer::scan_out`
  (the only call that makes the fbdev helper commit; splash 3.757 s, shell first frame), `FBIOBLANK` only on the saver (`panel.rs`, and one unblank at
  shell start, a no-op when not blanked). Never used: FBIOPUT_VSCREENINFO, VT_ACTIVATE, VT_SETMODE, DRM setcrtc on the framebuffer path (a test
  guards this). Ruled out: probe mode (Always and Full alike), the display probe, a framebuffer var change. Not ruled out: the Thunderbolt/T2
  side (the hub is behind a Thunderbolt 3 port; `thunderbolt` binds at 3.84 s, `bce_vhci` suspends at 3.78 s) or another event at 4.5 s
  (the log line `sound output: ...` has the same millisecond but only reports a result). New: `iyakki.fbset=skip|always` (default always) leaves
  the pan out; the log shows `console: ... KDSETMODE skipped|set` and `fb: FBIOPAN_DISPLAY done ... mode fields unchanged|CHANGED by it`.
  Mac tests 2026-11-04 refuted: thunderbolt blacklisted (drop at 4.637 s), apple_bce blacklisted (4.538 s), probe off/always/full (still drops),
  so Thunderbolt, T2 and the probe are not the cause; the shell's display setup comes after the drop. The drop is always 0.57-0.8 s after
  "splash: whole frame written" and, in image 1104c, 88 ms after "splash: stopped after 15 frames" (4.458 s -> 4.546 s). What the boot screen
  thread does (code audit, `backend.rs` splash_loop): open /dev/fb0 (O_RDWR, mmap), FBIOGET_VSCREENINFO/FSCREENINFO (read only), KDSETMODE 1,
  writes into the mapping about 20 times a second, one FBIOPAN_DISPLAY with the current offsets after the first whole frame (3.757 s), sysfs reads
  of the DRM connectors; at its end it munmaps and closes /dev/fb0 (the LAST close of the fbdev node before the shell reopens it at 4.60 s) and
  leaves the console in graphics mode. No FBIOPUT_VSCREENINFO, FBIOBLANK, VT or ANSI writes. Candidates, unproven: (a) the munmap + last close
  of /dev/fb0 at 4.46 s (nearest event to the drop; `iyakki.fbset=skip` now leaves it open and logs it); (b) the pan at 3.757 s (0.8 s before;
  `iyakki.fbset=skip` skips it); (c) the fbcon/console state after KD_GRAPHICS; (d) an event outside our code at 4.5 s (a keyboard on hub 1-8.2
  enumerates at 4.44-4.59 s in 1104c). New: `iyakki.bootscreen=off` skips the boot screen thread completely (one log line); a once-only hub
  re-attach 30 s after boot (`touchprobe::step`) when no finger event was ever seen, the hub has no disk below it and is not a root hub,
  log line "touch: no finger event was ever seen from ...; its USB hub ... was switched off and on once".
  Check next on the Mac: boot with `iyakki.fbset=skip` (picture may stay dark on i915; touch is the question); if touch still drops, boot the
  same kernel with `initcall_blacklist=` / `thunderbolt` blacklisted, and with `usbcore.autosuspend=-1`; compare the disconnect time with the
  first line any of our processes writes at that time (`dmesg` plus `/var/log/iyakki-boot.log`). Boot loader hand-over: iyakki-boot/docs/PLAN.md 3b.
  Software replug (new, `touchfix.rs`, unverified on the Mac): once per boot, at 45 s or later, only for a USB touch panel with no finger event ever
  seen and nobody typing in the last 5 s, after the hub re-attach: rung 1 `link` writes `off`, 1.5 s, `on`, `detect` to the connected
  `/sys/class/drm/card*-DP-*/status` (the screen blinks for 2-4 s; `detect` is always written last, also from a guard); 6 s wait for a finger event;
  rung 2 `xhci` unbinds and binds the xHCI controller the panel hangs off (PCI address from its sysfs path, e.g. 0000:00:14.0; every USB device,
  keyboard and mouse too, re-enumerates) only if rung 1 did not help. `iyakki.touchfix=off|link|all` (default all). Log lines to compare boots:
  `touch: iyakki.touchfix=...`, `touch: fix rung 1 (link)...`, `touch: fix rung 2 (xhci)...`, `touch: fix after rung ...: finger event seen|not seen; panel re-enumerated|not`,
  `touch: fix ended: ...`. Check on the Mac: boot with touchfix=link, then all, then off, and compare those lines with the first finger event.
  Recovery for a touch screen that goes silent while running (detection, a ladder of rebind and reset steps, rate limits, a Settings -> Keyboard "Reset touch screen" button): `docs/development/TOUCH-RECOVERY.md` (F-DISP-10). Tested with fakes only; probably not enough for this DisplayPort case, since the hub drops before our code runs. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the long text above is the history of the hunt. The cause was found on 2026-11-07: the panel's controller comes up in mouse mode after a cold boot with the cable connected, so only its mouse-like interface speaks, and the shell muted that interface because a multi-touch device was present.
