The shell now lets the mouse-like interface speak until the multi-touch device has spoken (`iyakki-shell::input::tests::mouse_like_interface_speaks_until_the_touch_device_does`); **verified on the Mac mini on 2026-11-07 (image 1107i, update pack, full power off, cold boot, owner touching): touch worked** (docs/development/TOUCH-RECOVERY.md, "VERIFIED"). What is still open: the panel behaves as a single-finger screen until a cable replug (no multi-finger gestures), and the USB hub drop at about 4.5 s after the boot screen was never shown to be fixed (the shell now works around its effect). **Hardware check:** cold boot with the cable in, tap and drag (expect the log line "controller in mouse mode"), then try a two-finger scroll or pinch; replug and try again.
* KI-078 **The macOS desktop window (fixed in alpha.3 for the build; a few tiles are limited on a Mac).** Found 2026-11-10: `./run.sh check-macos` failed with about 180 errors from Linux-only code without a `cfg`. Fixed 2026-11-11: the code compiles and the window runs on a Mac mini (arm64, macOS 27; Rust only, no other tools); Linux-only calls sit behind `cfg(target_os = "linux")` or the agent's small `oscompat` module, with stand-ins that fail with "not available on this platform"; the `check-macos` row of `alpha-check` is now FAIL-level when the Rust target is installed. Window mode is always desktop mode (the window launcher says so: cloud mode was switching on from empty `/sys` facts and hid RADIO, MEDIA, TALES, VOICE, CAMERA and DISKS). Sound: a native AudioQueue sink for the mixer (checked on the Mac: the system consumed every frame queued; whether it is heard needs a person listening). BROWSER: the full engine helper is a Linux script, so the built-in reader is the browser and the start page says so. State folder `~/Library/Application Support/iyakki-dev`. What is limited on macOS window mode: Linux apps (containers need namespaces), VPN, Wi-Fi hotspot and sharing, developer-mode patching and device nodes, touch-screen recovery, USB, Bluetooth, cameras (no video4linux), real disk management, AAC and HLS radio (the audio add-on is a Linux program; MP3 streams use the built-in decoder), the full web engine, the microphone (VOICE can read aloud only through an installed speech engine), wake phrase. Not verified: sound actually heard, a live radio stream and a live web page on the Mac, mouse and keyboard in the window (the SSH session cannot send input), Retina scaling, and the tests of `iyakki-agent` on macOS (made portable in 2026-11-12 where the difference was in the test, e.g. real folder paths, an append rule, an arm64 asset name; tests that need Linux hardware folders are skipped on macOS with a reason). The Linux images, QEMU and the Linux window are unchanged. (F-WIN-*, docs/INSTALL.md, docs/RELEASING.md) **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** reworded above. Not verified: sound heard, a live radio stream, mouse and keyboard in the window, Retina scaling.
* KI-079 **Tests that failed now and then in a full parallel run and passed alone (found 2026-11-10; cause found 2026-11-11).** `domains::...the_firewall_opening_is_its_own_step_with_local_scope_an_end_time_and_goes_with_disabling` (about 5 of 20 full runs), `domains::...resume_starts_a_listener_left_on_and_suspend_stops_it` and `vpn_dns::tests::dropping_the_runner_stops_the_thread_and_frees_the_port` (run 16 of the gate) all failed with "Address already in use" on a port taken from `testdir::free_port`, and all three bind the same number a second time (restart or rebind after a stop). **Cause (test helper, not the product):** the cross-process claim in `testdir::free_port` (a file with the process id) had a race: a process that found a fresh, still empty claim file of another process deleted it as "stale" and wrote its own, so two test processes (two gate runs on one machine) were both handed the same port and one took it between the other's stop and rebind. A test that starts 6 copies of itself at once and compares the ports they hold failed in 15 of 15 tries with that scheme. `iyakki-sshfs` also had its own copy of the helper with no claim at all and the 16-bit wrap-around, in the same port range; and the helper probed TCP only while the DNS forwarder test uses UDP too. **Fixed:** the claim is now an exclusive `flock` on the port file held until the process ends (no stale file to judge, no takeover race), the sshfs copy uses the same claim folder, the probe covers UDP, and the "port free again" test waits (bounded, 2 s) for the number instead of one immediate try. After the fix the same cross-process test passed 30 of 30 tries and the domains and vpn_dns tests passed in 60 of 60 runs under CPU load with 4 test processes at once. **Not proven:** the failures could not be reproduced with a single process under load (none in about 70 runs before and after), so the cross-process cause is the only one found that fits all three (same run, same message, rebind of a number); the three tests are removed from the flaky list and `dist/release/test-with-rerun.sh` will list them again if they come back. `k8sretry::tests::the_feed_keeps_the_last_good_list_then_shows_the_banner_and_recovers` is a separate timing test; its cause was found on 2026-11-11 (next paragraph). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** fixed in the test helpers; not re-run by me. If a test fails now and then in a full run, add it to this entry. Separately, on 2026-11-12 one tile test (`events_in_and_a_display_list_out_over_tls_on_a_loopback_port`) failed once in a busy run and passed alone; its helper now retries the loopback request twice.

  **Second round (2026-11-11, cause found for each; tests fixed, not the product, except one small product race).** Method: the agent lib tests were run 6 times in full, then 3 and 4 more times under CPU load (4 to 6 `yes` hogs, other builds on the machine, load average 15 to 22), and every module that failed was looped 30 to 60 times with 8 processes at once. Before the fixes every one of the 6 plain full runs failed (1 or 2 tests each time). Causes: (1) `k8sretry` ... `recovers`: after the list was stored the feed thread asks for the namespaces next, and `status()` reports the namespace tracker once the list is fine, so the test sometimes looked in between (failed 2 of 60 loaded runs; now waits until the status is clear, with the fake clock moved on if the namespace call was in flight, and prints the log on failure; 0 of 140). (2) `webmix` page-sound lag (the one unnamed test that failed in a full run; 5 of 6 full runs): a real-time measurement of the mixer thread and the pipe on a normal-priority thread, so a loaded machine adds its own scheduling delay (91 ms on an idle machine, 120 to 505 ms loaded); the bound is unchanged and the test now adds the scheduling delay it measured during the run (a 5 ms sleep probe and the longest pause of the mixer thread) and prints both. (3) `pending_tests` (4 tests, 3 to 19 of 30 loaded runs), `quickinstall` start (9 of 30), `jobtools_tests` flow: the jobs were real `sleep 0.3` to `sleep 1` commands that ended while the test thread waited for a CPU; the jobs now wait for a file or a pipe that the test opens when it wants them to end. (4) `remotefiles` two-at-once: the fake server held only the first download at its gate, so whether the second was still Running when the test looked was a race (17 of 30 loaded); the gate now holds all that start. (5) `cloudlisten` per-tenant: the slot of the answered request is given back a moment after the client has the answer (2 of 30); the test polled one tenant and read the other once, now polls both. A second cloudlisten test looked at the forward being killed when its last reference went, which a finishing connection thread can hold for a moment; it polls (1 of 40 before). (6) `radio` tests: sound goes to the play command only while the global mixer is off, and any test that turns the mixer on (or listens for a page's sound) sent a radio test's sound to the mixer: "never got there: Playing {Test FM}" for the missing-player case, fixed by pinning the command route in the radio tests that name a play command (the machinery existed, `mixer::pin_command_route`, and `voice.rs` used it); the same tests also read the file `tee` wrote right after stopping the player (an empty file under load) and used a finite 40-frame stream that ends in milliseconds, so the state passed Playing and Failed between two looks: now they wait for the bytes before stopping and use an endless stream. (7) `hostvoice` pause/seek: a seek starts a new reading and the old one cancels its piece on its own thread, so the cancel reached the page after the new piece; the test waits for it. (8) `clientmedia` mixer end to end: the mixer mixed a short tone as fast as it could, closed its output, and the tee flushed the client queue (by design) before a test thread that was not scheduled for a moment pulled anything (3 of 4 loaded full runs, 1 of 60 alone); the mixer is now paced and the tone lasts 3 s. (9) `extagent::login` refused code (once in 4 loaded runs): a real small race in the product, not only in the test: the terminal reader stopped when the program had exited and one poll found nothing, but the program's last words could be written between that poll and the exit being seen, so the failure text sometimes lacked the reason; the reader now looks once more after it knows the program has exited. (10) `vpn_mgr` dropped tunnel: the test tried to bind the forwarder's port once right after the tunnel went away while the forwarder thread gives it back when it notices (once); it polls up to 10 s. (11) The shell test `make_runs_in_the_background...` ("a frame took 1.3 s", up to 3.4 s in the heaviest runs): the slow steps are now 4 s and the bounds 3 s, so a page that waited for a step is still caught but a loaded frame is not; `a_move_shows_its_progress...` (27 of 30 failed at the heaviest load: "progress steps seen: [Finishing]") now has 1.5 s steps (0 of 30). **After the fixes:** every module above ran 40 to 100 times at 8 processes at once under load with 0 failures, except one `radio` aac test (stopped `tee` before it had written, fixed afterwards, 0 of 40) and three runs of the shell make test at the very heaviest load. Full agent lib runs (4700+ tests) under load after the fixes: 4 runs, 1 clean and 3 with failures only in tests outside this pass (`camdetect` masked pixels in 2 runs; the two mixer speed tests in 1 run), none in the tests fixed here. **Not explained:** `domains::...the_firewall_opening_is_its_own_step...` failed once more in a loaded full run with "Address already in use" on a freshly claimed port even after the claim fix; `testdir::describe_port` now names the holder (process id and name, socket state) in that test's message, so the next occurrence will say who held the port. The mixer tests `mixer::stress_tests::the_heard_position_advances_smoothly_between_polls` and `mixer::tests::mixing_four_sources_for_ten_seconds_takes_far_less_than_real_time` (speed measurements) and `camdetect::watch::tests::masked_pixels_never_reach_the_recorder...` failed in one loaded full run each and were not changed (audio and camera areas were off limits for this pass). **Third round (2026-11-11):** the mixer speed test now measures the mixing thread's CPU time instead of the wall clock; the smooth-position test adds twice the scheduling delay a probe thread saw to its allowed step; the camdetect test waited for "two entries in all", which was already true on its second and third recording, so it now waits for two new ones; the enchome frame test fails on a second frame over 3 s, not the first; and the listener bind tries again for up to a second on "address in use" (a restart meeting its own closing socket). Each ran 20 times beside 6 CPU hogs and 4 other test processes (100 of 100 runs passed, domains 80 of 80); no full-suite run was repeated.
* KI-080 **Fixed in code (see KI-093), confirmation on the Lenovo pending.** **The camera light stays on after leaving the Cameras tile (found 2026-11-10 on a Lenovo laptop, alpha2c).** After pressing Back the camera is not released, so its light stays on. It was not seen with a single camera tile. The cause is not found. Alpha.3 now closes the computer's own cameras the moment the person leaves the Cameras tile (tested with a fake camera that counts open handles) instead of waiting for the 8-second idle time; that covers a slow close, but it is not confirmed on the Lenovo, so the row stays open until it is checked there (`docs/project/ALPHA-3-PLAN.md`, item 9). Until then: close the Cameras tile and check the light; sign out or switch the machine off if it stays on. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** KI-093 describes the complete fix (the shell closes the computer's own cameras the moment the tile is not on the screen, in any way it is left); the light going out has not been seen on the Lenovo. **Hardware check:** on the Lenovo open CAMERAS, leave by Back, by the Home icon, by F11 and by locking, and watch the light each time.
* KI-081 **Owner decision made, fix verified on a USB keyboard; the new mapping needs a try.** **Ctrl+Cmd+Q does not lock the screen in the Mac profile (found 2026-11-10, alpha2c on a Mac).** Ctrl+Cmd+L locks, Ctrl+Cmd+Q does nothing; the unit test passes on a fake key stream. Cause not proven on hardware. The shell's key code path treats Q and L identically (a test over every Command/Control side, driver-swap setting, Apple-keyboard detection and scan-code/sync/repeat events shows the same result for both), so the difference came from the keyboard state, not from Q: the chord only counted when the profile was Mac and the saved Option/Command swap setting matched what the driver really does, and Ctrl+Windows+Q was nothing in the PC profile. Ctrl+Cmd+L most likely worked through the older Super+L route (Automatic picked the PC profile, or the swap setting was wrong). Fixed in source (unit tests only): in the Mac profile either Command or Option with Control now locks, and in the PC profile Ctrl+Windows+Q locks too. Not verified on a Mac keyboard; if it still fails, `iyakki dev diag` (Settings -> Keyboard shows the profile and the swap state) and `iyakki dev logs --system` after pressing it. Use Ctrl+Cmd+L or the Power menu meanwhile. **Verified 2026-11-11 on the Mac mini 2018 with a USB keyboard (HS-HA305): the owner pressed both Ctrl+Cmd+Q and Ctrl+Cmd+L and both lock the screen (the audit log shows the lock). Fixed on hardware, a Mac keyboard of Apple's own was not tried.** **2026-11-11, by the owner's decision: Ctrl+Cmd+Q (and Ctrl+Option+Q) now SIGNS OUT, after a confirm card (Enter or y signs out, Esc or n stays), and Ctrl+Cmd+L (and Ctrl+Option+L) locks; in the PC profile Super+L locks and Ctrl+Windows+Q signs out. The verification above that Q locked is kept as history. The new mapping is unit-tested on raw key streams only and needs a try on a USB or Apple keyboard.** **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** as written: Ctrl+Cmd+L locks and Ctrl+Cmd+Q asks to sign out (owner decision 2026-11-11, RELEASE-NOTES-0.1.0-alpha.2.md). **Hardware check:** press both on a USB keyboard and on an Apple keyboard on the Mac; Q must show the confirm card, L must lock.
* KI-082 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** **A covered camera shows a bare black picture (found 2026-11-10, HP Pavilion 16).** With the laptop's physical privacy shutter closed, the Cameras tile found the camera, opened it and showed a black picture and a crossed-out icon, with no message and nothing in the log. Opening the shutter fixed it. Fix in source (alpha.3): when sampled pictures stay black (about 99 in 100 pixels below brightness 12) for 6 seconds in a row, the hub reports the state "Black picture" and the tile writes over the picture "The picture is black. Is the camera covered or the privacy shutter closed?" (a question, since a dark room can look the same; one lit picture clears it). Tested with synthetic frames only; NOT yet checked on the HP laptop with the real shutter, so the threshold and wording are unconfirmed on hardware. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** no change. **Hardware check:** on the HP Pavilion 16 close the privacy shutter with the Cameras tile open; after 6 s the question "The picture is black. Is the camera covered..." must show, and a lit picture must clear it.
* KI-083 **Fixed; verified on hardware for Quick install, other downloaders pending.** **A driver download with no network spins for a long time instead of failing (found 2026-11-10).** **Cause:** the downloaders waited for the long connect and stall limits (30 to 60 s) and then retried five times with growing pauses, so with no network a download took minutes before it said anything. **Fixed in alpha.3 (tested, not yet on hardware):** every install and driver download (Mac drivers from Apple, Camera firmware, firmware packs, Quick install, components) now starts with a quick reachability check of its host (name lookup and one connection, 4 s at most; skipped when a proxy is set) and, if it fails, stops at once with a plain message ("No network: ... Nothing was downloaded. Check the network cable, Wi-Fi or proxy, then press Retry (or Cancel).") without the retry pauses. Cancel is the Stop button in the installer list beside the clock; Retry is pressing the same button again (Mac drivers: Retry (no new download)). Tested with the real HTTP client against a closed local port, a name that does not resolve, a listener that swallows connections and a server that accepts and never answers (`iyakki-proxy` guard tests, `iyakki-macrecovery` tests). **Verified 2026-11-11 on hardware (the T7 stick on the Lenovo, build 0.1.0-alpha.3-dev4): the owner reports that the Quick install download now detects the network problem.** **Not verified:** the other downloaders (Mac drivers, camera firmware, firmware packs, components) with the cable pulled; a one-click Retry button on the failed row in the installer list (not built; the message names the button to press again); a stalled but connected server still takes the stall limit (up to about 30 s per try), not a few seconds. Row stays open until checked on hardware. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** as written (verified for Quick install on the Lenovo, 2026-11-11). **Hardware check:** pull the cable and start a Mac drivers download, a camera firmware download and a firmware pack; each must stop within seconds with the "No network" message.
* KI-084 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** **A "firmware missing" message for Bluetooth on the MacBook Pro 14,1 while Bluetooth works (found 2026-11-10).** Tether and audio work; the message names a missing firmware file that the chip does not need for these. Planned: make the text follow the real state (alpha.3 item 12). Where exactly the message is shown was not recorded; a photo of the screen would help. Change (alpha.3): the Bluetooth controller headline in iyakki-devctl btview.rs (shown by Settings -> Bluetooth and the Devices popover) said "ROM state, no firmware file" for any known chip without a patch file, even with a valid address and a working controller; it now says "working; optional firmware file not found" when the controller is configured with a valid address, and keeps the ROM-state wording only when it has no valid address. Unit tests cover both states. The "needs firmware" notice (btstate) already required a missing address. Hardware unverified: the exact place the owner saw it is still unknown, so other sites (bt_pan blocker, checkup, macfw notes) were read and left as they only fire when no controller or no valid address exists; confirm on the MacBook Pro 14,1. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** no change. **Hardware check:** on the MacBook Pro 14,1 open Settings -> Bluetooth and the Devices popover; the headline must read "working; optional firmware file not found" and no firmware-missing notice may show.
* KI-085 **Changed in code, not measured.** **Browser sound lags behind the picture with the mixer on, and plays on after the page stops (owner 2026-11-10; alpha.3 item 5).** **Cause found by reading the code (not measured on a machine):** the page's sound was a normal Media source of the mixer, so it was queued like a radio stream: a 64 KB pipe from the engine (about 340 ms), a ring of 6 s, a start level of 800 ms (400 ms after a dry spell), the mixer's 300 ms write-ahead and a 500 ms device buffer that the card fills before it starts. A page that writes as fast as the pipe allows (the engine's `null`-backed PCM has no clock of its own) kept all of them full, so the delay was the sum (seconds at worst) and, on stop, everything queued still played: the lag and the tail are one cause. **Changed (alpha.3, tested with a fake sink, NOT on hardware):** the browser source is now a live source (`Mixer::open_live_source`): the pipe is cut to one page (4 KB, 21 ms), the tap reads 5 ms at a time, the ring holds 20 ms and starts after one period, and while it is open the mixer writes 2 periods (40 ms) ahead and asks the device for a 40 ms buffer in 10 ms periods (the device is reopened once when the source arrives; the normal 500 ms buffer returns after the card has slept). Target 100 ms end to end, settable with the environment variable `IYAKKI_WEB_AUDIO_LATENCY_MS` (20 to 500). When the page sends nothing for 100 ms the ring and the pipe are thrown away (a pause or stop does not play on), and `webmix::drop_queued()` does the same at once (about 20 ms); it is not yet called from the shell when a tile is left. The pipeline has no picture timestamps, so there is no A/V sync correction beyond the bounded delay. Test: a 6 s stream from a page that fills every queue measured 91 ms worst case to the fake device (bound 110 ms), and a stop empties the ring and pipe within 60 ms. **Not verified:** the real delay on the Mac mini or any machine (aplay and the card may hold more than the model says; the T2 driver may refuse a 40 ms buffer, then aplay's default buffer is used and the delay is larger), crackle or late periods from the short write-ahead (Settings -> Sound shows late periods; raise the target if they appear), and whether GStreamer keeps picture and sound in step over this pipe. Row stays open until measured on hardware. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** no change. **Hardware check:** on the Mac mini play a page with sound and measure the delay (aim for under 100 ms), stop the page and check that the sound stops within about 50 ms, and read the late-period count in Settings -> Sound.
* KI-086 **Custom request headers in the full web engine are written but not proven (owner request 2026-11-11; alpha.3 item 13).** The web process extension `dist/webview/wpe-helper/webext.c` (hooks `send-request`, receives the rules as an extension snapshot from the helper) has only been syntax-checked against stub headers: the build host has no WPE WebKit headers, so it was never compiled against the real API, never loaded by a real web process and never seen adding a header. Open points to check on a device: the pkg-config name of the extension API (`wpe-web-extension-2.0` or `wpe-web-process-extension-2.0`), that `initialize-web-extensions` and `user-message-received` behave as read from the documentation, that `send-request` fires for sub-resources and for every redirect hop, that the engine copies headers into a redirected request (the extension removes what it added when the new address does not match), and that service/shared workers do not pass through the hook (their requests get no headers). The image build treats a failed extension build as a warning, so a wrong package name leaves the full engine without the headers rather than failing the image. The matcher in C is narrower than the in-house one on purpose (see `iyakki-agent::browserheaders` docs); the Rust and C matchers are tested against the same vectors. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-087 **The browser client page (Connect a client, EXPERIMENTAL) is tried in one browser only (2026-11-11; alpha.3).** The page behind the one-time link (`/connect`) ran end to end in headless Chrome 157 against the session endpoint on a plain local socket with a fake screen (add the device, sign in, picture, key, click, revoke), and its engine ran in node. It has **not** been tried over TLS or a cloud domain, in Firefox or Safari, on a real phone, or against the real shell on a machine. Known limits: (1) over plain http on a local network the browser gives the page no WebCrypto, so the device key is kept in the page's storage where any script of that page can read it; the page says so and a stronger key needs https; (2) the machine clamps its screen to at least 640x400, so a phone held upright shows a small picture, which you pinch to zoom; (3) no file exchange; sound, clipboard, a machine camera and the device's own camera and microphone are in the page too but are described in KI-089; (4) with "Reconnect without asking again" ticked, the password stays in that tab's memory until you disconnect, so a dropped connection does not ask again; untick it to be asked every time; (5) a very old Safari may block the page's own WebSocket under its content security policy. If the page misbehaves, switch Connect a client off in Settings, Remote access: the page and the socket then answer 404. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-088 **The window program (`iyakki-connect window`, EXPERIMENTAL) is tried on Linux with Chrome 157 only (2026-11-11; alpha.3).** It was run end to end in headless Chrome against the real session endpoint over TLS with a fake screen (pinned link, enrolment, sign in, picture, input, a second run by saved name with the program signing, a revoke forgotten). Not tried: macOS and Windows (the browser paths for them are only tested with fake file lists; the crate uses Unix file modes, so it does not build on Windows yet), Edge, Firefox, Safari (the last two get an ordinary window, not an application window), a visible window, a cloud domain, hardware. The device key is a mode-600 file in the configuration folder, so it is only as safe as the user's account; the operating system's key store (Keychain, DPAPI, Secret Service) is not used yet. The enrol form now says the program keeps the key when the page runs in a window (2026-11-11). The page fetches its engine without credentials, so the loopback server serves `/connect/engine.wasm` without the cookie (Host and Origin are still checked; the file is public on the instance). The pin in a Settings link is the certificate in `<data>/tls/cert.pem`; a machine whose web access uses another certificate gets a link whose pin does not match (the program then stops; use the link without `?pin=`), and a certificate that renews changes the pin. Row F-CLIENT-02. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.
* KI-089 **Sound, clipboard and camera/microphone in the browser client are wired into the shell but tried with fakes and in headless Chrome only (2026-11-11; alpha.3, EXPERIMENTAL, off by default).** Behind Connect a client the page has a Devices button: the machine's sound played in the browser (the machine's own speakers stay quiet unless you choose "here and on the machine"), two clipboard buttons (text only, 64 KB, one press one use), a machine camera picked from a list of the cameras you may view, and sharing this device's camera and microphone with the machine. What a person should know: (1) **a device must be given each thing first**: Settings, Remote access, Connect a client, `d` then the device number (or "Device permissions"), or ask the assistant (`client_devices`); the five switches are `audio.out`, `clipboard.client`, `camera.view`, `client.camera.offer`, `client.microphone.offer`; "on" only means allowed to ask; (2) **the clipboard and sharing the device's camera or microphone put a card on the machine every time** (Allow this time or Deny, over the screen of the person the device is signed in as; nobody answering in 60 seconds is a no, and developer-mode input cannot answer it); (3) **whose sound**: the machine has one mixer, so the sound goes to the client of the person who signed in last, and only while they are; with several people signed in only that one hears it on a client; a client of someone else gets nothing and the machine's speakers are not silenced for it; (4) **what the device shares is accepted and thrown away**: nothing on the machine uses a client's camera or microphone yet; the machine's red sign says "X camera is in use by this machine" while one streams (the existing REC sign, one sign), and the page shows its own red band; (5) the camera list shows the cameras the person may view, by name; with no camera feed (a cloud pod) the machine answers "unsupported"; (6) the clipboard "from the machine" is the text you last copied inside IYAKKI during this session, and "to the machine" is pasted where you are typing; (7) the sound plays 40 to 100 ms behind and is not delayed to match the picture. Tried: fakes for the card, the sound card, the screen and the camera (the card was drawn and answered in the shell's test rig at five window sizes); node for the audio queue and the picker; one run in headless Chrome 157 with its fake camera and microphone. Not tried: a real phone, Safari, Firefox, speakers, a webcam, the Mac mini, TLS, a live chat using the new tools, the window program with sound or a camera. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** still true.

* KI-090 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** **The DASH tile's "Local cluster (k3s)" dashboard sometimes shows "This panel could not be loaded {…" on the "Claims not bound" panel (Mac mini, alpha.2 image from 77ebeca, found 2026-11-11 by `dist/hwtest/hwcheck.py run F-DASH-06`).** Steps: DASH tile, open the first dashboard (Local cluster (k3s), 20 panels), go back to the list and open it again; about 1 open in 4 to 6 shows the red error card on that one panel (the other 19 panels load, and `iyakki dev kube get pvc -A` answers at once with all claims Bound). Refresh clears it. The message is cut to `{…`, so the person cannot tell why. Not found yet: whether the stat query (`stat_pvcs_not_bound`) hits a timeout or reads a half answer. Screenshots: `~/iyakki-work/hwtest/2026-11-11/F-DASH-06/panel-error-intermittent.png` and the `panel-error-open*.png` files beside it. Feature row: F-DASH-06 stays partial. **Follow-up 2026-11-11 (read-only).** No log line records the error: `dev logs --system` shell, audit and engine and `dev dmesg --grep dash` hold nothing about the panel, so the failure cannot be seen from outside. From the code: this panel runs `kubectl get pvc -A -o json` (`dash/cluster.rs` `KubeRead::Pvcs`, read by `s_pvcs` in `dash/kube.rs`) like the other 19 panels of that board, which all start at once on worker threads; the shared JSON reader answers 'kubectl did not answer with JSON (the answer may have been cut because it was too large)' for a cut or empty answer, yet the card shows only `{…`, so another message is being cut. Not proven; the likeliest cause is a timeout or cap while about 20 kubectl runs share the managed k3s, but no run was timed. Suggested next steps for whoever fixes it: log the panel's error text, show the whole message in the card, and run the shared reads once per refresh instead of once per panel. **Cause and fix 2026-11-11 (in code, not yet on a device).** Cause found by reading `k3sdev.rs` `checked` and the runner: a kubectl run whose exit status was taken by the PID-1 reaper (`status_lost`, code unknown, output whole), and any run cut by its 20 s timeout or the size cap, was turned into an error whose text was the (partial or whole) JSON answer; the card showed its first line, `{…`. About 20 panels start at once, so some runs lose the race. Fix in `dash/cluster.rs`: `kubectl_result` reads a whole answer with a lost status as success and names the cause otherwise (timeout / invalid output / refused / kubectl missing); at most 4 kubectl runs at once (`MAX_KUBECTL_AT_ONCE`, each with its own timeout); one automatic retry on a transient error; a panel error never shows a raw `{`. Tests: `dash::kube_tests::twenty_panels_at_once_use_at_most_four_runs_and_show_no_error`, `one_cut_answer_is_retried_once_before_an_error_shows`, `a_cut_answer_that_keeps_failing_names_the_cause_not_a_brace`, `kubectl_result_names_each_cause`. **To re-check on the Mac mini:** open Local cluster (k3s) 20 times; no red card. Not done: logging the panel error text. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the fix and its four tests are in the tree and pass; the Mac mini has not re-run it. **Hardware check:** on the Mac mini open DASH, Local cluster (k3s), 20 times (`dist/hwtest/hwcheck.py run F-DASH-06`); no red card on "Claims not bound".
* KI-091 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** **Settings -> Dev cluster -> Platform services says Gitea is "not installed" and the Registry stays on "checking…", while the cluster runs both (Mac mini, alpha.2 image from 77ebeca, found 2026-11-11 by `hwcheck.py run F-APPS-50`).** Steps: Settings, search "dev cluster", Platform services (the Private store row says "not installed", which is right: there is no `iyakki-store` namespace). `iyakki dev kube get deploy -A` shows `iyakki-gitea gitea 1/1` and `iyakki-registry registry 1/1`, Store -> Setup shows Gitea "Running", and the page still reads Gitea "not installed" and Registry "checking…" after Look again and 60 s. The row looks only at its own install records, not at the cluster (not confirmed in the code). Screenshot: `~/iyakki-work/hwtest/2026-11-11/F-APPS-50/platform-services.png`. Feature rows: F-APPS-50 and F-K8S-18 stay partial. **Follow-up 2026-11-11 (read-only, from the code).** The page does not ask the cluster about Gitea or the private store: each row is 'not installed' when the signed-in account has no install record for that service (`installed::load(dir, user)` in `apps/platform_services.rs`, `reload`); a Gitea put in by Store -> Setup (quick install, namespace iyakki-gitea) has no such record, so it reads 'not installed' although it runs. The Registry row comes from a background probe that needs the account's saved cluster choice (`devcluster::Choice::load`); with no saved choice the probe yields nothing and the row stays on 'checking…' for ever (`registry_up` is only set by `Done::Status`). Both are consistent with what was seen (no `devcluster-<user>.json` was looked for: the files scope was withdrawn from this computer during the run). The audit log holds only my clicks on Platform services, nothing from the probe. **Fix 2026-11-11 (in code, not yet on a device).** The rows now ask the cluster: `clusterprobe::probe` runs one read-only `kubectl get deploy -A` (3 s request, 5 s in all) through the existing runner in the page's background job, kept 5 s; a service the cluster runs but the account has no record of reads "running (installed outside Store)"; the Registry row ends on "installed", "not installed", "no cluster chosen" or "cluster not answering (timed out / reason)", never "checking…" for ever. Tests (fake runner): `clusterprobe::tests::installed_elsewhere_is_seen`, `absent`, `store_namespace`, `timeout_is_named`, `refused_names_the_reason`, `no_choice_finishes_at_once`. **To re-check on the Mac mini:** Settings, Dev cluster, Platform services with Gitea from Store -> Setup. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the fix and its six tests are in the tree. **Hardware check:** on the Mac mini with Gitea installed from Store -> Setup, open Settings -> Dev cluster -> Platform services (`hwcheck.py run F-APPS-50`); Gitea must read "running (installed outside Store)" and the Registry row must end on a real state.
* KI-092 **Fixed in the code, to be re-measured on the Mac mini. The shell draws about 6.7 frames a second on a Home screen that does not change (Mac mini, alpha.2 image from 77ebeca, found 2026-11-11 by `hwcheck.py run F-SHELL-13`).** Steps: sign in, stay on Home with no input for 45 s, read `iyakki dev dmesg --grep "shell:"`: every 10 s line says 66 to 67 frames, draw 28 ms, screen write 1.4 ms, and two screenshots taken seconds apart are byte-identical; PID 1 uses 8 to 9 CPU seconds in 30 s (about 28 % of a core). The row F-SHELL-13 says "redraw only when something changed". The SYSTEM tile idles at about 7.4 frames a second too. Cause not found (something asks for a frame about every 150 ms; the dev connection is not the cause, the count is the same with no `dev` command during the window). Evidence: `~/iyakki-work/hwtest/2026-11-11/F-SHELL-13/` (frame lines, the three screenshots, PID 1 CPU seconds). **Follow-up 2026-11-11 (read-only, from the code).** The heartbeat is every 33 ms (`shell.rs`, `tick`); a frame is drawn only when `stale` is set. Setters that run on every tick or every 5th tick: `dev_state_tick` sets `stale` for 5 s after every `dev state`/`diag`/screenshot request (`devmode/state.rs` `WANT_SECS`), so a paired computer that polls more often than every 5 s keeps the shell drawing at full rate; the idle count of 66-67 frames per 10 s (one frame about every 150 ms, i.e. every 4.5 heartbeats) does not match that (full rate would be near 30 a second) and was measured with no dev command for 45 s, so another periodic setter is the likelier one (a 5-tick timer such as `permpop_tick`, `startup_tick` or the devices popover refresh are the candidates; none was proven). The frame lines in the kernel log (`shell: N frames in 10s ...`) do not say which tick asked. A cheap next step: count stale-setters by name in `FrameStats`. **Second machine, 2026-11-11:** on the Mac mini 2014 (Macmini7,1, image from g2197e69) the idle Home draws 54-56 frames per 10 s, draw 68 ms each, slowest 114-154 ms, and PID 1 used 13 CPU seconds in 30 s (about 43 % of a core). Evidence: `~/iyakki-work/hwtest/2026-11-11/macmini2/F-SHELL-13/`. The same on two machines and two builds points at the shell loop, not the hardware. **Third measurement, 2026-11-11 (Mac mini 2018, build 0.1.0-alpha.3-dev4, `hwcheck.py --device macmini-disk run F-SHELL-13`).** Idle Home: 63, 63 and 66 frames per 10 s, draw 34 to 38 ms each, slowest 51 to 60 ms, PID 1 used 10 CPU seconds in 30 s (about 33 % of a core), two screenshots byte-identical. Same pattern as on the first build, so the dev4 build did not change it. Evidence: `~/iyakki-work/hwtest/2026-11-11/macmini-disk/F-SHELL-13/`. **Cause found 2026-11-12 (harness).** Not a `stale` setter: the backend's safety-net redraw. `redraw_interval_ms` returned 100 ms for every screen that is not an app, and the loop draws when that time has run since the last frame, so an idle Home was drawn every 100 ms plus the draw time (a harness of 300 idle heartbeats: 76 frames, 73 of them `timed`, 2 from the clock text, 1 from the start-up strip; 7.5 a second, the same as the 6.7 measured on the Mac mini). **Fix:** new `home_is_calm` (Home, signed in, no card, popover, toast or sign, start-up strip and the developer-mode request pulse not animating, no camera sign): the safety net is 500 ms there; anything moving keeps 100 ms, and every real change (click, badge, strip text, clock minute, request) still asks for its own frame. Instrumentation kept: every `stale = true` in the shell now goes through `mark_stale!`, which counts the source line, the backend counts `timed` frames, and the 10 s frame line in the log ends with `by reason: shell.rs:2083=2, timed=20, ...`. Dropping a pixel-identical present was not added: `present_banded` already compares rows against the last scene and writes nothing for an identical frame, so only the 28 ms draw was wasted. `dev_state_tick` still redraws for 5 s after a `dev state` or screenshot request (wanted: the screenshot needs a fresh frame). **Tests:** `shell::idle_tests` (unit/harness level: 300 idle heartbeats on Home draw at most 25 frames and name the culprit on failure; a busy strip keeps 100 ms). **Not measured on the Mac mini:** re-run `hwcheck.py run F-SHELL-13` and read the idle frames per 10 s (expect about 20) and PID 1 CPU seconds per 30 s (was 8 to 9). **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** headline already says so. **Hardware check:** `hwcheck.py run F-SHELL-13` on the Mac mini; expect about 20 idle frames per 10 s and PID 1 at about 2 CPU seconds per 30 s (was 8 to 9).
* KI-093 **The CAMERAS app (several cameras) is slow to let the computer's own camera go; the CAMERA app is not (found 2026-11-11, T7 stick on the Lenovo, build 0.1.0-alpha.3-dev4). Fixed in the code, to be confirmed on the Lenovo.** The owner: the single CAMERA app puts the camera light out at once when it is left; the CAMERAS app takes noticeably longer. Cause: the CAMERAS hub keeps a stream alive for 8 seconds after the last look, and KI-080 released it only on Esc and q inside the tile. Every other way off the tile (the Back button, the Home icon, F11, another tile or the inbox, a desktop view switch, a pane or layout change, the lock, the sign-out, the screen saver) ended the looks but left the stream, and so the light, running until the 8 seconds were over; a page or layout that drew fewer cameras, the add form, a refused or unplugged camera and a revoked broker grant did the same. Fix: (1) the shell checks after every event and heartbeat whether the tile is on the screen (the shown app or a pane, signed in, not locked, no saver) and closes the computer's own cameras in the same event when it is not; (2) the tile tells the hub after every drawn frame which cameras it drew, and the others close at once; (3) a look that is refused (unplugged, no right, broker said no) closes the running stream; (4) the hub now knows who looks: the screen's looks and every other holder's (a connected client, the assistant's camera tools) are kept apart, so letting go of the screen closes a camera only when no other holder looked within the idle time. No other holder of the computer's own cameras exists today: recording and the motion watcher never start one, and the snapshot reads the running stream. Network cameras are never touched. Tested with fakes only (unit tests in the agent, the tile and the shell with a camera that counts open handles); the light going out has not been seen on the Lenovo, so the row stays open. Known limit: the stream thread notices the stop at its next frame wait, up to 0.5 s on a camera that delivers no frames, and a very quick reopen can find the camera still busy for that moment. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** headline already says so; see KI-080 for the hardware check.
* KI-094 **Message and b43 noise fixed in code; the card itself still has no driver.** **Wi-Fi does not work on the 2013 to 2015 Macs with a Broadcom BCM4360 card (found 2026-11-12 on a Mac mini 2014, Macmini7,1; the owner's report plus the kernel log).** The kernel finds `Broadcom 4360 WLAN`, the old `b43` driver tries it and fails with `FOUND UNSUPPORTED PHY (Analog 12, Type 11 (AC), Revision 1)`, error -95, and `brcmfmac` does not support this chip, so there is no Wi-Fi device. The usual fix on other systems is the Broadcom `wl` driver, which is closed source and not allowed here. **What works:** the cable, a USB Wi-Fi adapter (the MediaTek and other firmware packs exist), a phone's tether over USB or Bluetooth (verified on other machines). **Not done at first (both done in the follow-ups below):** keeping `b43` from probing the card (a harmless but noisy error at every boot); naming the machine in Settings, Network so the person is told why there is no Wi-Fi. **Follow-ups done 2026-11-11 (in code; the modprobe file needs the next image).** (a) `dist/buildroot/board/iyakki/overlay/etc/modprobe.d/iyakki-broadcom-unsupported.conf` blacklists b43, b43legacy, brcmsmac, bcma and ssb so `b43` no longer probes the card (no `FOUND UNSUPPORTED PHY` line from the next image; it is a file, not a kernel change; listed in `legal/image-rules.txt`). (b) Settings -> Network (the WiFi row) and the `w` join message say "This computer's Wi-Fi card (Broadcom BCM43xx, 2013 to 2015 Macs) has no driver that IYAKKI can include. Use the network cable, a USB Wi-Fi adapter, or your phone's tether (USB or Bluetooth)." when sysfs shows PCI 14e4:43a0, 43a1, 43a2, 4360, 4352 or 43b1 (`iyakki_devctl::broadcom_wifi_without_driver`). Tests: `iyakki-devctl::tests::a_broadcom_4360_family_card_is_found_from_sysfs`, `iyakki-shell::apps::devices::tests::a_broadcom_4360_card_without_a_driver_is_named_on_the_wifi_row`, and the join message in `settings::tests::a_hidden_network_a_missing_adapter_and_an_ordinary_account_are_handled`. **To re-check on the Mac mini 2014 and the MacBook Pro 2018 (T2 Macs may use a different card): boot the next image, no b43 error, and the Network page shows the sentence.** **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the two "not done" items are done in code; what stays true is that there is no Wi-Fi driver for this card (Broadcom `wl` is closed source and not allowed here). **Hardware check:** boot the current image on the Mac mini 2014 (and a 2018 T2 MacBook); `dmesg` must have no `FOUND UNSUPPORTED PHY` line and Settings -> Network must show the sentence.
* KI-095 **Fixed in code, hardware check pending.** **People's home folders and files are world-readable: a developer-mode shell (user `nobody`) can list and read `/home/admin` although the `files` scope is withdrawn (Mac mini 2018, build 0.1.0-alpha.3-dev4, found 2026-11-11 by `hwcheck.py run F-STORE-09`).** Steps: with the owner's standing shell grant, `iyakki dev --device macmini-disk shell -- ls -ld /home/admin` shows `drwxr-xr-x root root`, `ls /home/admin` lists the person's folders (Downloads, Pictures, Dashboards, Flows, `.claude`, diagnostics files) and `test -r /home/admin/iyakki-diagnostics-20261111-214035.txt` succeeds (`-rw-r--r-- root root`). The same holds for `/home/scratch` and `/home/scratch2` (755). `dev whoami` says `files` is NOT held, yet the shell scope reads the same folder as `nobody`. The row F-STORE-09 says a home is made 0700 on first use; these folders were made earlier (or by the encrypted-home mount, whose root is 755), and nothing tightens an existing home, and files inside (diagnostics, downloads) are created 0644. The vault, passwords and keys were not reachable (the data folder `/var/lib/iyakki` refused with Permission denied). Not found: whether other local users (the k3s pods run with hostPath volumes) can read it too. Suggested: set 0700 on every `/home/<name>` at the bind (and on the encrypted-home mount root), create new files 0600 where they hold the person's data, and decide whether a shell without the files scope should see homes at all. Evidence: `~/iyakki-work/hwtest/2026-11-11/macmini-disk/F-STORE-09/home-bind.txt`. Feature row: F-STORE-09 stays partial. **Cause and fix (2026-11-12):** every IYAKKI account runs as root today (the agent and the shell are root; only a dev shell command runs as the account's system user or `nobody`), so the only protection the architecture allows is the folder mode: a home is `drwx------ root`, which `nobody` and any other unprivileged process cannot traverse, whatever the files inside say. The 0700 rule only ran for a missing home (`ensure_home`), so earlier homes stayed 0755. Now `scope::tighten_home` (open with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY`, `fchmod` through the descriptor: no link is followed, nothing outside the folder is touched, idempotent) is applied (1) at every start by PID 1 after the `/home` bind (`scope::tighten_homes`: real folders directly under `/home` on the same file system only, one audit line per change in the boot log) and (2) whenever `user_home`/`ensure_home` is asked for an existing home. Files inside are not rewritten (the folder mode shields them; a bounded walk was judged not needed); an encrypted home mount root was already 0700. New files: diagnostics, media downloads, browser screenshots are created 0600 (dev `files` pushes and camera recordings already were). Tests: `scope::home_tests::existing_homes_are_tightened_without_following_links_or_leaving_the_folder` (0755 home tightened, files inside kept, symlink and outside folder untouched, second run empty, new home 0700), `a_missing_home_is_made_private_and_only_on_the_system`, `diagshare` file mode, and `devmode::tests_adb::files_stay_inside_the_sandbox...` (the granted `files` scope still pushes, pulls and lists through a 0700 home). Boundary gate unchanged (no new libc touchpoint rows needed). **Re-check on both Mac minis after the next image:** `iyakki dev --device <name> shell -- ls -ld /home/*` shows `drwx------`, and a read as `nobody` (`ls /home/admin`, `test -r /home/admin/<file>`) is refused with Permission denied; the boot log has a `home: ... now 0700` line. Not tried on a device. **Re-checked 2026-11-12 against the code, FEATURES.md and the changelog:** the fix and tests are in the tree. **Hardware check:** after the next image on both Mac minis, `iyakki dev --device <name> shell -- ls -ld /home/*` shows `drwx------` and a read as `nobody` is refused (`hwcheck.py run F-STORE-09`).

* KI-120 **The interactive SSH login of the CLI tile was never tried against a real server (found 2026-11-12 while finishing F-CLI-08).** The tile, the key forwarding and the `ssh_login` assistant tool are tested with a stand-in `ssh` program on a real pseudo-terminal (Linux earlier, macOS 2026-11-12), not with OpenSSH or Dropbear talking to an sshd, and `vi`, `top` and `tmux` through it were not tried. Until the 2026-11-12 change a login ran the command `true` and so ended at once on a real client; it now asks for a terminal (`-t`). Keys only: a server that asks for a password cannot be reached this way. Steps to check: save a connection, trust its host key (T on the Connections tab), press L, run `top`, resize the window, press F10. Reference: F-CLI-08, docs/CLI-SSH.md.
* KI-121 **A panic in a thread an app started ends that thread, and a few places still turn a poisoned lock into an error (found 2026-11-12 while finishing F-SHELL-40).** Locks in the shell, agent, core and toolkit code are now taken poison-tolerant, so one panic no longer makes every later user of the lock panic. A thread that panicked is not restarted and its work is not retried, so the app may show an old state until it is opened again; sign-in still reports a "lock" storage error when the account store's lock is poisoned (`store.lock().map_err(..)`), and code in other crates (flow host, store server, webview) was not converted. Reference: F-SHELL-40.
* KI-122 **Dragging a finger over the Store Setup rows does not scroll them as the About and Linux apps lists do (found 2026-11-12 while finishing F-SETUP-06).** The shared list helper has the body drag, but a press on a Setup row opens its card at once, so the drag cannot start there; the touch screen's own finger scroll (a drag that starts at once) still works. The other lists of the shell were not converted either. Reference: F-SETUP-06.
* KI-140 **No script run in the full web engine (F-BROW-41, M21):** the Developer tools prompt and the Elements tab work for pages the built-in reader shows. The full engine runs in a helper process with a fixed line protocol that cannot evaluate a script and return a result, so there they say so. A report about a full-engine page has the host and engine only, no notes.
* KI-141 **Dialog titles are announced for six tiles only (F-A11Y-02, M21):** with the screen reader on, EDIT, DATA, DISKS, INBOX, BROWSER and STORE say the title of a dialog they open (`App::dialog_title`). Other tiles that draw their own dialogs (for example CHAT, SETTINGS forms, IDE, DASH) now get a generic announcement (M44 2026-11-13): the tile name and the buttons of the modal frame, for example "Store. Install, Cancel"; the exact title of those dialogs is still not said. Tried with a fake voice only.
* KI-142 **Assistant opens a download (F-BROX-01, M21):** `browser_download_open` queues a request that the shell takes on its next event; it was tested with the chat tool and the shell separately, not with a model in a live chat. If no event arrives (a locked screen) it waits. MEDIA playback was not heard.
* KI-170 **The mouse and touchpad speed setting was never tried on a real mouse or touchpad (found 2026-11-12 while adding F-KBD-20).** The scaling, the Settings page, the chat tools and `iyakki dev config set pointer.speed` are covered by unit and page tests with synthetic events. How 25 % and 300 % feel on a 4K screen with a real mouse, and whether a touchpad that the kernel lists as a relative device should follow the touchpad speed instead of the mouse speed, is open. Workaround: Settings -> Keyboard, Pointer speed; `[` `]` step the mouse by 5 %.
* KI-180 **Automatic sound output did not follow a monitor hotplug for minutes (found 2026-11-12 on the Mac mini 2018).** A monitor was unplugged from HDMI and the same one plugged in over USB-C; the sound kept going to the removed output and the owner had to choose the new one by hand in Settings. Causes found in the code: an output chosen by hand was kept even when its HDMI/DP pin reported no monitor, and the watcher only noticed changes of the card and PCM lists (the monitor pins were not part of what it compared) and could trust a jack control that was still "on". Recovery added: the watcher reads the pins' ELD files every 500 ms, takes a full look at once and two settle looks after a change, a pin without a monitor is set aside (Automatic takes the next choice; a chosen output falls back and returns), and the mixer closes on the new generation. Tested with made-up `/proc/asound` trees only; **hardware re-test needed** on the Mac mini (unplug and replug a monitor, also over USB-C, and watch `sound output:` in the log and the sound itself).
* KI-200 **A lean stick's loader menu named a RAM disk that was not on it, so nothing started (found 2026-11-12 on a Lenovo laptop with the T7 stick).** The loader showed "IYAKKI was not started: initrd /boot/initrd cannot be read" (twice in `last-boot.log`). The stick was lean (`boot/initrd-lean`, `boot/root.sfs`, no `boot/initrd`) but `EFI/iyakki-boot/boot.cfg`, written by an update push, still said `initrd /boot/initrd` on 23 rows: the update pack carries the loader menu of the image it was made from, `dev update` copied it unchanged, and unlike GRUB (which picks `initrd-lean` when it is there) the loader reads exactly the file a row names. The owner repaired the stick by hand. Code fix (M34): every write of the menu goes through `iyakki-storage::bootcfg` (the update, the installer's lean-free copy), an update repairs a menu that names a missing disk even when it changes nothing else, and a patch file lets the loader try the `-lean` or full sibling itself. Tested with fake boot volumes only; the stick has to be updated and booted again on the Lenovo to close this, and the loader patch is not in any image yet.
* KI-210 **The Devices popover showed a laptop panel as `2560x1600 @ 0 Hz` (found 2026-11-12 on a Lenovo Legion Pro 5 with an AMD GPU; fixed in code, hardware re-check needed).** The refresh rate was read from the framebuffer's mode line (`/sys/class/graphics/fb0/modes`), which says `U:2560x1600p-0` on amdgpu, and applied to every display. Now the number is used only when it is not 0 and the size is that output's; otherwise the preferred timing of the connector's EDID is used when its size is the output's mode; if nothing knows the rate, the row prints no `Hz` at all (never `0 Hz`). The KMS renderer's mode struct also had `vrefresh` 0 from such drivers: `ModeInfo::refresh_hz` works it out from the pixel clock and the totals (interlace, double scan and vscan included) and the real KMS backend fills the field from it. Tested with made-up timings, EDIDs and `/sys` trees only; **hardware re-check needed** on the Lenovo (Settings -> Devices popover, Display row, should show the panel's real rate).
* KI-260 **`iyakki dev shell -- ls` failed with "could not start the command: Permission denied" on a Lenovo after a reboot (found 2026-11-13).** An IYAKKI account is not a system user, so the shell runs its commands as `nobody`; its folder was the account's home, which KI-095 had made `root:root` 0700 at that start, so the command could not even enter it. Fixed in code (hardware re-check needed): the folder is now checked as the user the command runs as, and a folder that user cannot enter becomes that user's own home (`/tmp/iyakki-dev`, made private to it); a home owned by a real user means commands run as that user; the 0700 repair hands a root-owned home to its passwd user, and leaves a root-owned home of an account with no system user as it is (so that account's own commands can still enter it). Not yet seen on the device: the same dev shell after a reboot with the new image. The account store holds no user number, so there is none to use.
* KI-400 **Holding Fn does not cycle the Touch Bar strips (F-KBD-14, M44):** the chip at the left cycles them by touch, but the Apple keyboard's Fn key is handled by the kernel driver and nothing shows yet whether it reaches the shell as its own event; it needs the keyboard to try.
* KI-401 **The scheduled backup is not encrypted unless a passphrase file is named (F-BACKUP-01, M44):** an unattended run cannot ask for a passphrase and one never goes through the chat. The page, the status and the chat card say so; a backup to a network share works only when the share is already mounted as a folder.
* KI-402 **Command+C in the IDE terminal never stops a wait, but that path has no test of its own (F-KBD-19, M44):** the Mac profile flag that tells Command+C from Control+C is tested; the IDE terminal's use of it is not.
* KI-560 **Registering the boot volume as a firmware boot entry has never run on real firmware (F-NET-17, M53):** `iyakki startup-disk register | first | next` and the restart-into-IYAKKI setting write the UEFI variables `Boot####`, `BootOrder` and `BootNext` through efivarfs. Tested only on fake variable folders and a fake disk. Whether a Lenovo or other firmware accepts the short-form device path of a removable stick, and honours `BootNext` after a restart from developer mode, is not known; it needs a real try (`iyakki startup-disk status` shows what the firmware holds).

<!-- appendix:end -->
* KI-600 **The kernel and the NVIDIA module carry the build machine's account and computer name (found 2026-11-13 on a Lenovo, `/proc/version`).** The banner reads `(user@host)` of whoever built the image. Fixed in the build scripts (`dist/buildroot/build.sh` sets KBUILD_BUILD_USER/HOST and puts `dist/buildroot/build-identity` first on PATH) and checked by `dist/release/image-identity-scan.sh`; the images built before this commit still carry the name, so the kernel and the NVIDIA module must be rebuilt before any public image (release gate: run the scan on the kernel image).
* KI-823 **The Chat tile cannot choose tools, resume history or answer a question (F-WEB-53, P-C, 2026-11-13).** It chooses among the models the service lists and stops a run; a coding agent is chosen on the Chat page.

* KI-822 **The Edit tile saves files of up to 12 KiB, one at a time, through a stand-in-tested path (F-WEB-54, P-C, 2026-11-13).** The route tests used a stand-in tool set; the real `read_file` and `write_file` were not run through a browser tile on a real machine.

* KI-821 **The flows runner is the first slice (F-WEBSVC-12, P-C, 2026-11-13; Q-D 2026-11-14).** `data-api` exists; `tile-host` exists only as a first slice that serves the built-in WASM tiles (list and events), with no route to answer the approvals a tile asks for. Not built: installing tile packages, a tile's page and the `window.iyakki` bridge over WebSocket (the web-bundle tile runtime of the plan).

* KI-863 **The data API was tried through a stand-in tool set (F-WEBSVC-12, P-C, 2026-11-13).** The three data tools behind `data-api` were not run against a real data file through a real service in a test; `tile-host` and the answer-a-step route do not exist.

* KI-862 **No headless Buildroot image and no release-size figure (F-WEBSVC-11, P-C, 2026-11-13).** The container recipe `build-cloud.sh docker --headless` exists and is checked, not built; the ISO and disk images have no headless variant.

* KI-861 **The Settings tile changes two switches only (F-WEB-56, P-C, 2026-11-13).** Tested with a stand-in tool set, not with the real Settings tools in a browser.

* KI-860 **The Runs tile answers only the first question of a run at a time (F-WEB-55, P-C, 2026-11-13).** Not tried in a real browser; it holds the `runs.decide` word, which no other tile may be given without the person approving it.

* KI-820 **The headless build was only run on macOS and is not wired into any image (F-WEBSVC-11, P-C, 2026-11-13).** It is 46.5% of the full binary in the `fast` profile; the agent still links its web engine crates, and the release profile was not measured.

* KI-766 **The Chat tile is a small chat (F-WEB-53, narrowed Q-D 2026-11-14).** It has a model choice, Stop and a list of earlier conversations; no tool choice, no attachments, no formatted answers, no voice; questions are answered in the approvals list. Tested in the sandbox and through the routes, not on a screen.

* KI-765 **The Edit tile is a small editor (F-WEB-54, narrowed Q-D 2026-11-14).** Files, syntax colours and find exist; no assistant proposals or diffs, no pointer selection or clipboard.

* KI-764 **The Settings tile is an overview only (F-WEB-56, P-C, 2026-11-13).** It changes nothing; every setting stays on the Settings page.

* KI-763 **(closed 2026-11-13 by F-WEBSVC-11 step 2: see KI-820.)** There was no headless build; there is one now.

* KI-762 **The Runs tile is read only and was not tried in a browser (F-WEB-55, P-C, 2026-11-13).** It lists and shows runs; it cannot stop one or answer a question, and the page-form Runs page stays. Tested in the sandbox and through the routes, not on a screen.

* KI-761 **Custom resource instances have no page in the K8S tile (F-K8S-26, P-C, 2026-11-13).** Only the data layer exists, tested against a fake kubectl; exec, port-forward, applying YAML and the tile icon are not built.

* KI-760 **Shared folders: no HTTPS, no .local name, no all-interface IPv6, no assistant tool (F-SHARE-07, P-C, 2026-11-13).** Only the IPv6 loopback listener and bracketed links exist.

* KI-730 **The new web pages and the audit paging have never run in a real browser (P-C, 2026-11-13).** The Chat page, the Cloud access audit section, the event-stream refresh of Runs and Flows were run under node 22 with stand-in pages and a fake API, on a Linux test host (the Rust tests skip without node, so the gate does not run them). No Chrome run was done.
* KI-731 **`/metrics` has a small set of numbers (P-C, 2026-11-13).** Requests, time, sign-ins, sessions, connections, runs held, uptime; none of the run, approval, tool, token, flow-run, memory or disk counters of the plan, and only administrators may read it.
* KI-736 **SSH server: no RSA host key, no P-521 login keys (F-FILE-14, P-C, 2026-11-13).** P-384 login keys work (tried with OpenSSH 10.5 on a Mac). P-521 keys are refused because `ring` cannot check them; clients with only an RSA host key still cannot connect; the Settings page does not show the ECDSA host fingerprint.

* KI-735 **The remote runner has no Settings page and was only tried against a scripted or in-process service (P-C, 2026-11-13).** Saved machines are managed by chat tools only; the other machine is reached over plain http on loopback in tests, https with a private CA is not supported, and no command-on-another-machine runner exists.

* KI-734 **The S3 dashboard source was never run against a real object store (P-C, 2026-11-13).** Its signatures equal the AWS documentation's examples and the rest ran against a scripted fake; no MinIO or AWS server was asked. The installed object store is not registered as a source by itself.

* KI-733 **A tile call that asks first has no card of its own in the browser (P-C, 2026-11-13).** The question is listed and answered through the common approvals list and route; the cards in the pages are the ones for runs, and no page test drives a tile question. No real tile uses it yet.

* KI-732 **The editor module for the browser has no page (P-C, 2026-11-13).** `iyakki-edit-wasm` loads and works under node; nothing in the web UI uses it yet (the Web Edit tile needs the tile mechanism of F-WEB-04).

* KI-640 **(closed 2026-11-14, Q-D) A parallel task's transcript (F-IDE-10).** The Tasks tab now has a Chat tab beside Log with the run's conversation; it is kept in memory while the shell runs, so after a restart an earlier run's conversation is read from its run log (Runs).
* KI-641 **Not every list of the shell uses the shared scrolling helper (F-SETUP-06).** The About page, the Linux apps lists and the Store Setup tiles scroll by wheel, pad, bar, finger drag and keys; other lists still have their own scrolling.
* KI-642 **The snapshot key source has no settings page and no chat tool (F-CLOUD-15).** It is set in the tenant launch configuration (`snapshot_keys`) or on the snapshot command line. A cloud KMS or HSM client is not written: the `Kms` trait is where the operator's own goes.
* KI-643 **The firewall does not read the tenant's policy (F-TCFG-09).** The tenant egress policy narrows the egress proxy; the firewall rules are addresses and ports and are not made from it.
* KI-644 **The namespace sandbox tier does not run in a pod (F-SBX-03).** The executor and its arguments are tested with a fake launcher; the pod's security settings that let the user namespace work need a real cluster to find out.
* KI-645 **HTTP/2 is not served by the domain listener (F-CLOUD-11).** The frame codec exists; HPACK (with the Huffman table of RFC 7541) and the stream state do not, and ALPN offers http/1.1 only.
* KI-646 **Team API-key integrations were never called against a real provider, and a personal Claude or Copilot sign-in cannot be shared (F-TCFG-11).** Needs a live key; sharing a personal login is an owner and licence decision.
* KI-680 **Enterprise and WEP WiFi entries are written but have never joined a real network (M58, 2026-11-13).** Settings asks for the login (or the WEP key with a warning) and the supplicant file gets the block, tested on made-up scans and stand-in programs. Nothing ran with a real adapter or access point, and the portal probe and signal read have timers (`wifi_ext::LinkTimers`) but no loop in the shell calls them yet. Passwords for these networks sit in the owner-only `wifi.json`, not the vault, because the network must come up before anyone signs in.
* KI-681 **The tunnel's policy-routing text, the Bluetooth NAP server end and the IPv6 beacon field are pure code that nothing calls (M58, 2026-11-13).** `iyakki-vpn::policyroute`, `iyakki-devctl::bnep_nap` and the `ip6=` beacon line are tested on text and bytes only; there is no helper that applies the routes, no L2CAP listener or TAP bridge for phones, and no IPv6 socket. The aarch64 kernel has no policy routing.
* KI-682 **Address-and-port firewall sets are written but switched off (M58, 2026-11-13).** `BuildOptions::concat_sets` is false by default and nothing sets it. The set text was accepted by a real 7.0 kernel in a scratch namespace and the bytes equal what nft 1.1.6 sends, but our own bytes were never loaded into a kernel. Rules from different person-made rules have different notes, so only one rule that expands to many addresses and ports (a host name with several answers) collapses.
* KI-683 **The hotspot's country and WPA3 choice are saved and passed on, but a card whose handshake runs on the computer stays on WPA2 (M58, 2026-11-13).** The SAE exchange is not written on the computer; only a card that runs it itself is announced as WPA3. The country request was never tried on the Mac's Broadcom card.
* KI-690 **The WiFi chat tools and the live signal line have never run with a real adapter (M61, 2026-11-13).** They were tried with stand-in `iw`, `wpa_supplicant` and `udhcpc` programs and a local socket. The sign-in check sends nothing unless `wifi-probe.txt` names an address; no default address was chosen (owner choice). A sign-in button that opens the address in the browser is not written.
* KI-691 **LUKS2 is read but not mounted by IYAKKI (M61, 2026-11-13).** Our code opens a cryptsetup-made LUKS2 header and returns the master key (checked against a real image), but the Disks tile still runs `cryptsetup` to open a drive; nothing hands our key to device-mapper yet.
* KI-620 **A flow's browser steps are reported to the waiting-work ledger, but this was only compiled and read, not run (M55 2026-11-13).** `AgentHost::browser` wraps `run_steps` in a `WorkGuard` (Source Browser); no test drives the node through the browser engine, and a flow with many browser steps makes one ticket each, which could wake the chat once per step when the person asked to be told. Check on a device with a flow that has browser steps; if it is noisy, report only the run's first step.
* KI-621 **The Assist settings page and the Activity page were checked by tests at five sizes, not looked at on a real display (M55 2026-11-13).** The whole-flow trial from the Activity page runs read-only steps (an HTTP GET reaches the network); it asks nothing on the page because the person pressed Try. The chat tool `flow_trial` asks first.
* KI-622 **Choosing a camera by name is tested with a fake machine, not with two real cameras (M55 2026-11-13).** `AgentCamera::photo_from` matches the listed name (`video2: Desk Camera`) in any case; with a browser camera the browser's own list is used. The number in the listing (`video0`, `video2`) can change when a camera is replugged, so a flow that names the model is steadier than one that names the number.
* KI-700 **The assistant's phrase tables for German, French, Spanish and Hindi are short and were not read by a native speaker (M62 2026-11-13).** `crates/iyakki-agent/src/phrases.rs` holds starts of refusals, parts of refusals, promises to report back and denials; they were tested with sentences written by the model that made them, not with a real model answering in those languages. Since M66 the ask-first and the progress-claim guards read the same four languages (stems of read-only and change verbs, not a grammar: a rarer verb form is missed). Add languages and phrasings in that one file.
* KI-701 **Two tests of this checkout fail on a Mac (found 2026-11-13 by M66), unchanged since `integ/round4` 3583120 (checked on the unmodified tree).** `iyakki-agent::jobtools_tests::the_working_folder_must_be_inside_the_scope_and_inside_the_workspace_in_the_ide` (`job_start` with `true` is refused) and `iyakki-agent::ragtools_tests::another_persons_collection_is_invisible_and_unreadable` and `...::adding_a_folder_shows_the_folder_and_count_checks_the_scope_and_ingests` (the scope check on a folder under the temporary folder, which is `/private/var/...` on a Mac). They are probably the Mac's path and program layout, not the code; they were not looked at further, and the rows that cite them (F-RAG-08) were not raised.
* KI-702 **The tests `backend::a_shape_change_under_a_still_pointer_erases_the_old_and_paints_the_new_pixel_exactly` and `backend::the_hand_over_a_link_in_the_browser_reaches_the_screen_as_a_hand` end the test program without a result on a Mac (found 2026-11-13 by M66), the known macOS crash of the `backend::` tests.** F-SHELL-47 cites them, so its Test level stays 30 until they run (on the Linux gate).
* KI-703 **`apps::cameras::tests::motion_through_the_shell_keeps_the_key_in_the_owners_vault_shows_the_red_sign_and_the_sign_stops_it` failed once in a long run on a busy Mac and passes alone (M66 2026-11-13).** The same kind of load-time flake as the two Flows tile tests fixed in M62; not looked at further.
* KI-704 **`feature_matrix::matrix_is_consistent` fails on `integ/round4` 3583120 before any M66 change: F-VPN-08 cites nine `iyakki-vpn::filter_tests::*` tests, and the crate has no `filter_tests` (found 2026-11-13 by M66).** The VPN filter code or its tests did not come through a merge; the row was not touched by M66.
* KI-705 **KI-701 and KI-702 were test-side bugs and are fixed (M70 2026-11-13).** The temporary folders of `jobtools_tests` and `ragtools_tests` are now canonical paths (on a Mac `/var` is a link to `/private/var` and a read scope is compared with real paths), so the three tests pass on a Mac and on Linux; the framebuffer of a test (`fb.rs`, a screen over a Vec of the test) no longer unmaps heap memory when it is dropped (this ended the `backend::` and `fb::` tests with SIGSEGV on a Mac). KI-704 was fixed by the owner's merge. KI-703 (one cameras test failing once under load) is still open.
* KI-706 **Two `iyakki-usb` tests fail on a Mac because they call Linux programs (found 2026-11-13 by M70):** `gpt_install_tests::a_gpt_install_on_a_512_byte_disk_is_complete_and_verified` runs `fdisk -l` (the Mac's `fdisk` has no `-l`) and `planner_tests::a_windows_disk_is_read_with_types_labels_filesystems_and_free_space` reads a FAT file system information sector the Mac's tools do not write the same way. Not code bugs; they are green on the Linux gate. They should skip where the program is missing; not done yet. F-INST-14 does not cite them.
* KI-720 **F-CORE-23 is only partly done (P-B, 2026-11-13).** Golden fixtures now exist for three binary formats of finding F-8 (delta `IYBDELT1`, volume header `IYAKVOL1`, recording `IYAKREC1`; `tests/compat/alpha3/`). Q-A 2026-11-14: fixtures for backup `IYAKKIBK` (plain and sealed) and snapshot `IYSNAP1` now exist (`backup-v1-*.hex`, `snapshot-v1/`). Still owed: fixtures for wake weights, the knowledge base, the vault, the ledger and the bundle and wizard manifests; the `min reader version` header rule helper (F-8); the open enums with an `Unknown` case (F-5, touches `clientsession.rs` and the client engine matches); the developer-mode state and the other pattern-A files (F-9); the rustdoc-JSON surface scanner (F-10).
* KI-721 **F-CORE-24 has an engine but no way to reach it yet (P-B, 2026-11-13).** wizardrun.rs runs a spec against a fake Env in tests; no screen draws the steps, no chat tool lists, answers or cancels a wizard, the real Env (net.reach, path checks, capability.held, the migration runner of F-CORE-25) does not exist, and installing, updating or removing an app does not start a wizard. * KI-722 **F-CORE-25 is a library without its triggers (P-B, 2026-11-13).** bundlesnap.rs can snapshot, restore and run a migration with rollback, but the store update does not take a snapshot by itself, no WASM migrator runs in the tile sandbox, and there is no chat tool to list or restore a snapshot. * KI-723 **F-UPD-01: no download of a bundle, and no official release key (P-B 2026-11-13, narrowed Q-D 2026-11-14).** A signed .tgz is now opened from Settings -> System update (key b) and by the assistant (`updates_bundle_open`), checked with the release key this build has and listed for the usual Install click; there is no channel address to download one from, and the OFFICIAL release key and address are the owner's to give (without a key an update file is refused).
* KI-722 **F-CORE-25 is a library without its triggers (P-B, 2026-11-13).** bundlesnap.rs can snapshot, restore and run a migration with rollback, and (Q-A 2026-11-14) the store update now copies an item's data first and the assistant can list and restore the copies (`store_data_snapshots`, `store_data_restore`); still no WASM migrator runs in the tile sandbox, and nothing runs a migration automatically after an update. The migration code itself (what a wizard step named in its spec does) is supplied by the caller and none ships yet.
* KI-723 **F-UPD-01: the bundle reader has no entry point yet (P-B, 2026-11-13).** sysupdate.rs checks and unpacks a signed .tgz, but no command (`iyakki dev update-bundle`), Settings page or chat tool opens one, the channel address and the official release key are the owner's to give, and handing the staged folder to the apply step (with the approval and restart) is not wired.
* KI-724 **F-SDK-15: no k3s backend for apps, and the dash path is untested against a real source (P-B, 2026-11-13).** iyakki.k3s still answers Unavailable: a per-app cluster namespace needs the service package kind (the plan comes from the deploy lint). dash.query was compiled against the real engine but only run with a fake port, so a real Prometheus or SQL source has not been queried through the bridge.
* KI-725 **Rows waiting for the owner (P-B, 2026-11-13).** F-CORE-17 (the owner-run relay, after the beta), F-SDK-16 (whether any payment provider is ever wired), and the channel address and official release key for F-UPD-01.
* KI-726 **Rows that need hardware to prove (P-B, 2026-11-13).** F-BOOT-14 and F-BOOT-15 (real firmware and boot timeline), F-CHECKUP-04 (a GPU and USB devices), F-SDK-14 (a screen and a speaker), and microphone recognition for F-SDK-16.
* KI-727 **F-UPG-07 waits for the beta tag (P-B, 2026-11-13).** The beta-made data volume does not exist until then.
* KI-728 **F-CORE-18 and F-CORE-26 not started (P-B, 2026-11-13).** F-CORE-18 changes the client wire protocol in three places together and needs a compatibility window and a browser run; F-CORE-26 needs a broker store-app session to enforce member capabilities.
* KI-729 **F-ARCH-05, 06 and 07 not started (P-B, 2026-11-13).** Step 5 touches shell.rs and the settings tiles (home-screen and settings work) and adds a crate that changes the lockfile, the SBOM and the dependency gates; steps 6 and 7 depend on it.
* KI-740 **Cookie rows are left out only when the cookie file can be rewritten whole (F-VAULT-09, narrowed Q-D 2026-11-14).** The rows of a site left out are removed with the in-house SQLite writer (`sqlmini`); a cookie file with an index, a view, content in its write-ahead log, no host column or a format it does not read is kept as it was, so a never-kept site's cookies can still reach the sealed file then. Not tried on a real WPE cookie file.
* KI-741 **The browser-data choices have no Settings control (F-VAULT-07 to F-VAULT-10).** They are on the Browser page of Settings since 2026-11-13 (keys Z, L, Y, B, D and buttons) and by the chat tools; checked through the real app at five sizes, not looked at on a screen or by touch.
* KI-742 **Sealing at the lock screen reaches the focused app and the panes, not other displays (F-VAULT-08, narrowed Q-D 2026-11-14).** The browser closes its full engine before the data is sealed; an engine in an app on another display is not told. Not tried with the real WPE engine on a lock.
* KI-743 **The echo canceller is not in the audio path (F-VOICE-36).** There is no tap of what the mixer plays to give it as the reference; it was only tried on synthetic signals.
